Measured the actual rendered positions directly (Playwright `getBoundingClientRect()` against the graph container) and confirmed the guesses were off by several percentage points in both directions. Fixed properly — not by re-guessing closer numbers — by adopting this same codebase's own existing precedent for exactly this problem: `AnimatedHeading.tsx`'s `useLayoutEffect` + `getBoundingClientRect()` + a `window resize` listener, used there to measure real letter positions instead of hardcoding pixel offsets. `PlatformGraph` now measures each topic pill's real edge and the center card's real edges on mount (and on resize) and computes the SVG curve endpoints from those measurements, so the lines touch every pill and the card exactly regardless of text length, font rendering, or container width.

  - **Follow-up 3 (fix two real rendering bugs + add continuous motion, same session, before any PR):** user reported the graph looked "very static," lines were "uneven" with "very bold" stroke, and lines weren't connecting well to the center card. Investigated rather than just re-tuning numbers — found two real, distinct bugs, not style nitpicks:
    1. **Root cause of "uneven"/"bold" lines (and, found while fixing it, the traveling dots rendering as ellipses, not circles):** the SVG used a *square* `viewBox="0 0 100 100"` stretched with `preserveAspectRatio="none"` onto the container's *16:9* (`aspect-video`) box. Stretching a square coordinate system non-uniformly onto a non-square box scales x and y by **different factors** — which distorts anything that isn't pure position: stroke width renders thicker on steeper curve segments than flatter ones (the "uneven" look), and a perfect-circle `r` renders as an ellipse. Real fix, not a numbers tweak: made the viewBox's own aspect ratio match the container's (`VIEWBOX_WIDTH = 100`, `VIEWBOX_HEIGHT = (100 * 9) / 16 = 56.25`, both now exported from `graphData.ts`), with a `toSvgY()` helper converting the existing percent-of-height values into this corrected SVG-space scale at every point a y-coordinate enters a `<path>` `d` attribute or a `<circle>` `cx`/`cy`. Verified via `getBoundingClientRect()` on each traveling dot that width and height are now identical (confirming true circles, not ellipses) — not just a visual screenshot guess. Also added `vectorEffect="non-scaling-stroke"` + `strokeLinecap="round"` to every line and lowered the widths (2px highlighted / 1.25px normal, now in real screen pixels, unaffected by container size) for a thinner, crisper look addressing "very bold" directly.
    2. **Root cause of "not connect well":** the `useLayoutEffect` measurement ran while the pills/card were still sitting at their Framer Motion `initial` (pre-entrance) transform — they were defined with a `y: 16` slide-in offset, and Framer Motion applies that transform synchronously on mount, before the layout effect's `getBoundingClientRect()` call. So every line was computed against the *pre-animation* position, not the *settled* position the user actually sees — a real measurement-timing bug, not an off-by-a-few-percent guess. Fixed by dropping the `y` offset from `fadeInVariants` (now opacity-only for the outer container, the pills, and implicitly the card, which never had its own offset) — position is now identical in both the "hidden" and "visible" animation states, so it no longer matters when the measurement runs. Also added a `requestAnimationFrame` follow-up measurement after the synchronous one, as a safety net against post-paint width shifts (e.g. web font swap).
    - **"Feel alive" additions**, kept deliberately small (continuous-loop budget similar to Feature 05's per-section count): a soft pulsing glow (`opacity`/`scale` loop) behind the center collections card, a small `Sparkles` icon doodle floating/rotating above it, and a small glowing dot continuously traveling along each of the two highlighted (gradient) curves — computed via a cubic-bezier point-sampler (`sampleLink` in `graphData.ts`, matching the same control points as the visible `linkPath` curve, so the dot visually follows the actual curve shape rather than cutting a straight line across it) rather than a naive straight-line `cx`/`cy` interpolation.
    - **File-size follow-through:** the fixes above (plus the new motion) pushed `PlatformGraph.tsx` to 273 lines, over `code-standards.md`'s 200-line limit. Split immediately rather than leaving it oversized: pure math/data (`graphData.ts` — topic/collection arrays, the bezier sampler, `linkPath`, `cardEntryY`, the new viewBox constants), the measurement logic (`useGraphMeasurements.ts`, a dedicated hook — same DOM-measurement idiom as `AnimatedHeading.tsx`), and the small repeated card UI (`CollectionsCard.tsx`, used by both the desktop graph and the mobile fallback). `PlatformGraph.tsx` itself is back down to 183 lines, just orchestration + JSX.
    - Re-verified: typecheck/lint clean on all 4 files; Playwright screenshots desktop/mobile × light/dark confirming thin even-width lines that visibly terminate exactly on both the pills and the card, circular (not elliptical) traveling dots, and the new glow/sparkle/dot motion all working; no new overflow.
  - **Follow-up 4 (`/recover` — fixed a real dashed-line + overflow bug, then added more motion, same session, before any PR):** user ran the `recover` skill against two visible defects: graph lines rendering dashed instead of solid, and the graph visually overlapping/bleeding into the bento cards below it. Diagnosed as Failure Mode 1 (a specific, isolated, traceable bug — not a polluted session or a wrong foundation) and found two real root causes from the immediately preceding follow-up's own changes, not new mystery bugs:
    1. **Dashed lines:** the curves used Framer Motion's `pathLength` draw-in (a `stroke-dasharray`/`stroke-dashoffset` technique sized to the path's length at the time it's first computed) on a `d` attribute that gets **recomputed live** from `useGraphMeasurements` — and the previous follow-up had just added a second `requestAnimationFrame` measurement pass as a safety net, guaranteeing the `d` updates again shortly after mount. The cached dash spacing (sized to the old geometry) no longer matched the new path's actual length, so the dash pattern repeated unevenly along it instead of drawing one solid line.
    2. **Overlap with content below:** the same follow-up had added `overflow-visible` to the SVG (to stop the center glow's blur from clipping) — which removed the clipping that would otherwise have contained any path geometry briefly extending past the box during a measurement update, letting the bad-looking dashed tail paint directly over the section below instead of being invisibly clipped.
    - Fix: dropped `pathLength`/`pathVariants` entirely from the curve paths (they're now a plain opacity fade via the same `fadeInVariants` already used elsewhere — geometry-independent, so it can never desync from a changing `d`), and removed `overflow-visible` so the container clips normally again. Confirmed before writing any further motion that this alone fixed both reported symptoms.
    - **Then, per explicit follow-up request, added two more motion features on top of the fix** (not separate bugs — new asks): (1) every line (not just the 2 featured/gradient ones) now has its own continuously traveling dot, each row given a distinct color via the existing 5-token hue system (`DOT_FILL_CLASSES` in `graphData.ts`: `fill-xp`/`fill-accent`/`fill-info`/`fill-premium`, one per row, mirrored left/right), each with a different `duration` and `delay` (`dotDelay` per line, offset between the left and right sides too) so none of the 8 dots move in lockstep — deliberately "not in sync," per the request; (2) a `CardBorderGlow` component — two synced layers (a blurred halo + a crisp core, same `animate`/`transition`) that continuously travel clockwise around the center collections card's perimeter via 5-keyframe `top`/`left` percentage cycles (corner → corner → corner → corner → back to start, `ease: "linear"`, `repeat: Infinity`), rendered as a sibling inside the same already-`position: absolute` card wrapper (which already qualifies as the containing block for its children, no extra `relative` class needed).
    - **File split, again:** the per-line path+dot logic was extracted into its own `GraphLine.tsx` (one component rendering both the `<path>` and its `<motion.circle>` for a single line, reused 8× instead of duplicating the JSX) — this also simplified `PlatformGraph.tsx` itself back down to 143 lines despite the added features, comfortably under the 200-line limit without needing the precomputed `leftHighlight`/`rightHighlight` consts from the previous round (every line, not just 2, now computes its own sample inline inside `GraphLine`).
    - Re-verified: typecheck/lint clean across all files; Playwright screenshots confirmed solid (non-dashed) lines, zero overlap with the section below, 4 distinctly-colored dots per side at visibly different positions along their lines (confirming independent timing), and 3 time-spaced crops of the center card confirming the border glow dot actually travels around the full perimeter (bottom edge → top edge → bottom edge across the captured frames) rather than just sitting decoratively in one spot; no new overflow.
  - **Follow-up 5 (`/review`, then fixed everything found, same session, before any PR):** ran the project's `review` skill against the finished feature (manually, since project-local skills aren't registered with the Skill tool). Found and fixed 6 issues, all confirmed resolved afterward — not just reported:
    1. **Critical, confirmed via a live Playwright console check (not assumed):** every one of `GraphLine.tsx`'s 8 traveling-dot circles threw `Error: <circle> attribute cx/cy: Expected length, "undefined"` once on mount (16 real console errors per page load, reproducible in every theme/viewport tested, including mobile where the desktop graph is `display:none` but still mounted). Root cause: each circle's `animate` drove `cx`/`cy` via a keyframe array, but `initial` only specified `opacity: 0` — no `cx`/`cy` baseline — so Framer Motion had no defined starting value to fall back on before the first real animated frame. Fixed by giving every circle an explicit `initial={{ cx: sample.cx[0], cy: sample.cy[0], opacity: 0 }}`. Re-ran the same console check after the fix: zero errors/warnings across both themes and both viewports.
    2. **Important:** `InterviewPrepMiniVisual.tsx` had its own local `COLLECTIONS` array, an exact duplicate of `graphData.ts`'s exported `COLLECTIONS` — same 4 canonical names defined in two places that could silently drift apart. Fixed by importing from `graphData.ts` instead.
    3. **Important:** `ui-rules.md`'s own written "1–3 small looping elements per section" rule was stale against `PlatformGraph`'s actual ~12 concurrent loops. Rather than silently leaving the doc wrong or stripping the motion the user explicitly asked for, added a **Confirmed exception** entry directly under that rule in `ui-rules.md`, naming `PlatformGraph` specifically and the reasoning (explicit, repeated user request for this one hero visual, not license to add more loops elsewhere by default).
    4. **Minor:** the decorative `<svg>` in `PlatformGraph.tsx` had no `aria-hidden`. Added it — its entire contents (lines, dots) are connecting-line decoration, not real content (the actual topic/collection names remain in plain accessible text elsewhere in the same component).
    5. **Minor:** `DOT_FILL_CLASSES[index]` would have silently returned `undefined` (no fill class, falling back to SVG's default black fill) if a 5th topic were ever added to `LEFT_TOPICS`/`RIGHT_TOPICS` without also extending `DOT_FILL_CLASSES`/`Y_SLOTS`. Fixed with modulo indexing (`DOT_FILL_CLASSES[index % DOT_FILL_CLASSES.length]`) so the 3 independently-sized arrays can never produce an out-of-bounds lookup, even though they're not type-linked.
    6. **Minor, flagged as pre-existing/systemic but fixed in scope for this feature anyway:** no `prefers-reduced-motion` handling existed anywhere in the codebase, and this feature meaningfully raised how much continuous motion runs on the homepage. Rather than building a global solution (out of scope for one feature's review), added `useReducedMotion()` (from `framer-motion`) to every component in this feature that runs a continuous loop — `GraphLine`, `CardBorderGlow`, and `PlatformGraph`'s pulse/sparkle — so each collapses to a single static frame (still visible, just not animating) when the OS-level reduced-motion preference is on, instead of looping regardless. Verified via a Playwright context with `reducedMotion: 'reduce'`: screenshot confirms the dots render as static (non-moving) accents, and the only console output is Framer Motion's own informational notice that it detected the preference — not an error.
    - Re-verified after all 6 fixes: typecheck/lint clean; full console check clean in both normal and reduced-motion modes; Playwright screenshots desktop/mobile × light/dark unchanged from the last known-good state (no visual regression from the fixes); no new overflow.

  - **Real bug caught and fixed before any verification, not after:** the first draft used `text-inverse` for heading/subtext and `bg-surface` for the button. Both are wrong for this component specifically — `bg-accent-darker` (this section's background) stays a dark teal in *both* light and dark theme, but `text-inverse`/`bg-surface` both flip polarity *with* the page theme (inverse: white→near-black, surface: white→near-black) rather than staying a fixed color — which would have made the heading/subtext nearly invisible against the still-dark background once dark mode was toggled. This is the exact same class of token-misuse bug already caught once in Feature 06 (`ProjectEditorMiniVisual`'s dark-mode text), caught here before writing any further code rather than after a screenshot revealed it. Fixed by using `text-accent-foreground`/`bg-accent-foreground` instead — `--color-accent-foreground` (`#FFFFFF`) is the one color token in `ui-tokens.md` that is *not* redefined inside the `.dark {}` block, so it stays white in both themes by design (it exists specifically for "foreground text on a colored surface," which is exactly this case). Verified via computed-style checks (not just a screenshot) that the heading renders `rgb(255,255,255)` in both themes before moving on.
  - **Verification artifact, diagnosed rather than assumed to be a real bug:** an automated check that jumped straight to this section via `element.scrollIntoView({ behavior: "instant" })` from page-load left it stuck at `opacity: 0`, which looked like a broken `whileInView` trigger at first. Reproduced the exact same instant-jump method against the already-shipped, working `TestimonialsSection` and got the identical stuck result — confirming this is a generic headless-browser/IntersectionObserver artifact of one large instant jump with no intermediate scroll frames, not a defect in this feature's code. Re-verified by scrolling through all 5 homepage sections sequentially with real pauses between each (matching how an actual user scrolls) — every section, including this new one, animates to `opacity: 1` correctly.
  - Verified: typecheck (`npx tsc --noEmit`) and lint (`npx eslint`) clean; Playwright screenshots at desktop (1440px) and mobile (390px) × light/dark confirming the full-bleed band, badge/heading/subtext/button contrast staying correct in both themes, and the button's `/login` href; mobile overflow check unchanged from the pre-existing `CompanyLogosStrip` baseline (`scrollWidth` 719 vs `clientWidth` 390 in this run — within the already-documented 440/510/627/719 fluctuation range, still traced to that same pre-existing element, not a new regression).
  - **Follow-up (background swap, same session, before any PR):** user didn't like the flat solid-teal (`bg-accent-darker`) background. Asked for a concrete direction rather than re-opening the original open-ended question; offered 3 options (rich near-black with teal as accent only / a teal-to-near-black gradient / drop the "dark section" idea entirely) — user picked the near-black-with-teal-accent direction, closer to Stripe/Linear's dark closing CTA bands.

Documented as a new section in `ui-tokens.md` ("Theme-Invariant Surfaces") and cross-referenced from `ui-rules.md`'s Dark Mode section alongside the existing Monaco-editor exception — this is now the second deliberate "always the same regardless of theme" surface in the codebase.
    - Swapped `CTASection`'s background from `bg-accent-darker` to `bg-cta-dark`, pulled teal back to an accent role only: a 1px top-edge gradient line (`from-transparent via-accent to-transparent`) and 2 blurred `bg-accent` glow circles sit on top of the near-black surface, rather than the surface itself being teal. The button kept its existing `bg-accent-foreground`/`text-accent-darker` treatment unchanged.

This is the generalization the original fix was meant to produce, confirmed by reuse on the very next change rather than by inspection alone.

**Last completed:** 09 Event Loop Simulator (Hero Version) — ran the `architect` skill before building. Resolved language/decisions up front with the user: "Hero version" means a single hardcoded scenario with the full standard control set (Play/Step/Step Back/Restart/Autoplay/Speed) — Feature 23 later adds scenario *switching* on top of the same engine, not more controls. Kept `BrowserChromeBar`/`PanelHeader` inside this feature's own `components/` rather than promoting them to `src/components/shared/` (rule-of-three — Features 10–12 will likely want the same chrome, but extracting for one current consumer would be premature). Replaced the static `HeroSimulatorPreview.tsx` mock entirely — `Hero.tsx` now renders the real `EventLoopSimulator` from `src/features/simulators/event-loop/`.
  - New self-contained feature module: `src/features/simulators/event-loop/` — `types.ts`, `data/scenarios.ts` (`EVENT_LOOP_FRAMES`, `EXECUTION_ORDER_BY_OUTPUT`), `hooks/useEventLoopSimulator.ts`, and `components/{EventLoopSimulator,BrowserChromeBar,PanelHeader,SimulatorPanel,CallStack,WebAPIs,MicrotaskQueue,TaskQueue,CodePanel,ExecutionOrder,SimulatorControls}.tsx`. Matches `architecture.md`'s explicit per-simulator file list (one file each for CallStack/WebAPIs/MicrotaskQueue/TaskQueue), implemented as thin themed wrappers around one shared internal `SimulatorPanel.tsx` rather than 4x-duplicated JSX — sharing within a single simulator's own folder doesn't violate the "no shared simulator engine across simulators" invariant.
  - **Real design decision, not diff-action replay:** `scenarios.ts` authors each of the 8 steps as a complete resultant panel snapshot (`callStack`/`webAPIs`/`microtaskQueue`/`taskQueue`/`consoleOutput`/`activeCodeLines`), and the hook just indexes `EVENT_LOOP_FRAMES[currentStep - 1]` — no runtime folding/replay of push/move/output actions. This makes Step Back trivial (decrement index, recompute nothing) and is the most literal possible compliance with the architecture invariant "step scripts are the source of truth, never derive steps from runtime computation."
  - **Play vs. Autoplay are genuinely separate controls**, per the build-plan's state shape listing both `isPlaying` and `autoplay` independently: Play runs the 8-step sequence through once and stops at the end; Autoplay (a `Switch`) makes it loop back to step 1 and keep going once Play is engaged. Verified via Playwright that toggling Autoplay before/while at step 8 and clicking Play correctly wraps back to step 1 and continues, while Play alone (autoplay off) stops cleanly at step 8.
  - **Mobile simplification required zero extra code:** per `ui-rules.md`'s "Hero simulator is simplified (no interactive controls) on mobile," the engine's default state (`currentStep: 1`, `isPlaying: false`) is already exactly "frozen at step 1" — hiding `SimulatorControls` via a plain `hidden md:block` wrapper was sufficient on its own, no `useMediaQuery` hook or mobile-specific component needed, since nothing can trigger a step change without the controls being visible.
  - **Verification caught a false alarm, not a real bug:** an early Playwright screenshot of the homepage top looked completely blank below the navbar (no headline, no simulator) after only an 800ms wait. Re-checked with `waitUntil: "networkidle"` + a 2.5s wait and it rendered correctly — confirmed as a first-hit Next.js dev cold-compile delay on the freshly-edited `Hero.tsx` route, not a runtime defect (no console/page errors either time).
  - **Verification method for per-step code highlighting:** the `bg-accent-muted` highlight color (`#F0FDFA`) is subtle enough that a compressed screenshot crop genuinely couldn't be read by eye with confidence about exactly which code line was highlighted at a glance. Used a direct `getComputedStyle().backgroundColor` check on every code line across all 8 steps instead of trusting the screenshot — confirmed the highlighted line(s) exactly match `scenarios.ts`'s authored `activeCodeLines` for every frame, same "measure, don't eyeball" precedent as `getBoundingClientRect()` checks used elsewhere in this project (Feature 04 logo sizing, Feature 06 `PlatformGraph`).
  - Verified: typecheck (`npx tsc --noEmit`) and lint (`npx eslint`) clean; Playwright across desktop (1440px) × light/dark confirmed Step/Step Back/Restart/Play/Pause/Autoplay-loop/Speed all transition to the correct frame (DOM text assertions, not just screenshots); mobile (390px) confirmed frozen at "Step 1 of 8" with controls hidden and no new overflow beyond the pre-existing `CompanyLogosStrip` baseline (`scrollWidth` 440 vs `clientWidth` 390); zero console/page errors in any check.
  - **Follow-up (4 fixes, same session, before any PR):** user reviewed screenshots and reported 4 issues — fixed all 4, 3 real component bugs plus 1 environment artifact:
    1. **`BrowserChromeBar`'s address pill wasn't horizontally centered** in the chrome bar — root cause: the bar was a plain `flex` row (traffic-lights, then a `flex-1 justify-center` div for the pill), which centers the pill within the *remaining* space after the traffic-lights' width, not within the bar's true full width, so the pill always sat visibly left of center. Fixed by switching to `grid grid-cols-[1fr_auto_1fr]` — traffic lights in the first (left-aligned) column, the pill auto-sized in the middle column, and an empty `aria-hidden` spacer in the third column so both outer columns absorb equal leftover space. Verified via `getBoundingClientRect()` that the pill's center now exactly equals the bar's center (0.0px diff), not just visually.
    2. **No literal "output" card existed** — only the Execution Order bar implied what had run. Split the previous full-width `CodePanel` row into a 2-column row using the same 4-column grid template as the panels above: `CodePanel` now `lg:col-span-3` (aligned under Call Stack/Web APIs/Microtask Queue) and a new `OutputPanel.tsx` (`lg:col-span-1`, aligned under Task Queue) — a small terminal-style card listing `consoleOutput` lines (`> start`, `> end`, ...) colored by the same per-origin theme already used by `ExecutionOrder`. Verified via DOM `closest('.rounded-lg')` measurement (not a screenshot guess) that Code's left edge and width exactly match Call Stack→Microtask Queue's combined span, and Output's exactly match Task Queue's span.

Investigated rather than assumed: confirmed via `git show --stat` that Feature 09's commit never touched `CTASection.tsx` or `globals.css`, and confirmed `--color-cta-dark` was still correctly defined inside `@theme {}`. Checked the live computed background-color and got `rgba(0,0,0,0)` (transparent) instead of the expected near-black — a real serving bug, not a misread screenshot. Diagnosed as the same stale-Turbopack-dev-cache class of issue already documented once in Feature 01 (`globals.css` changes not reflected after enough hot-reload churn across a long session). Fixed the same way: killed the long-running dev server, deleted `.next`, restarted clean — re-checked computed background and got the correct `rgb(10, 10, 10)`. Not a code defect; no source file changed for this one.

  - **Follow-up 2 (real correctness bug, same session, before any PR):** user caught that frames 7 and 8 showed the Promise `.then` and `setTimeout` callbacks "executing" directly inside the Microtask Queue / Task Queue panels, with the Call Stack left empty — explicitly asked me to confirm I understood the actual JS event-loop semantics before touching the fix. Confirmed and explained back: queues only ever hold a *waiting* callback; the event loop must dequeue it onto the Call Stack before it runs, since the Call Stack is the only place code executes — showing "Executing..." inside a queue slot teaches the architecture backwards. Fixed in `scenarios.ts`: frame 7 now empties `microtaskQueue` and puts `{ code: "console.log('promise')", status: "Executing..." }` in `callStack`; frame 8 does the same for `taskQueue` → `callStack` with `console.log('timeout')` — matching frames 1/2/5/6, which already only ever show "Executing..." inside the Call Stack panel.
    - Flagged but deliberately left alone (out of scope for what was reported): the Promise `.then` registration still shows up in the "Web APIs" panel alongside the real `setTimeout` timer (steps 3–6), inherited from the original Feature 03 static mock — real JS never routes promise microtasks through Web APIs (only the timer is a genuine Web API; a resolved promise's callback should sit in the Microtask Queue from the moment it's registered). Noted to the user as a separate, bigger design call; not changed without being asked.
    - Verified the fix itself via direct DOM text inspection of each panel (not a screenshot) across all 8 steps. Hit a confusing false read on the first pass — stepping forward with only 150ms between clicks showed step 6's Call Stack still containing step 5's item and step 7's Web APIs still containing the already-removed Promise item — but this was the test script outrunning the panels' own 300ms exit animation, not a real bug: re-running with 600ms waits (longer than the transition) showed every panel settle to the exact authored state, and a second pass with the browser's network cache fully disabled (`Network.setCacheDisabled` via CDP) on a fresh navigation confirmed the same correct result independent of any caching variable.
  - **Follow-up 3 (full event-loop accuracy rewrite, same session, before any PR):** user pushed back again on Follow-up 2's fix — having both callbacks jump straight from Web APIs to the Call Stack defeats the entire purpose of the Microtask Queue / Task Queue panels existing at all. User then provided a complete, explicit 11-step real-world execution trace (Web APIs → queue → Call Stack for both callbacks, microtask queue fully drained before the task queue is ever checked) and asked for confirmation of understanding before any further fix — same "let me know before fixing" gate as Follow-up 2. Confirmed back, including two corrections beyond the original 8-step scenario that the user's trace made explicit: (1) `setTimeout`'s callback enters the Task Queue as soon as its 0ms timer fires, independent of whether the synchronous script is still running — not only after the Call Stack empties; (2) `Promise.resolve().then(...)` in this scenario does **not** genuinely involve a Web API in real JS (it resolves synchronously) — the user's trace has it going straight to the Microtask Queue, which is more accurate than the original static mock's choice (inherited from Feature 03) to show it sitting in Web APIs alongside the timer. Flagged one deliberate non-change: no explicit "Global Execution Context" Call Stack frame, since the simulator's Call Stack panel has always only ever shown real user-code statements, never engine-internal bookkeeping frames — kept that abstraction rather than adding new fidelity nobody asked for.
    - **Immediate follow-up before implementing:** user added one more deliberate, explicit deviation from real `Promise.resolve()` semantics — since real frontend code almost always uses `fetch()` (a genuine Web API) rather than a synchronously-resolving `Promise.resolve()`, the user asked for the Promise's path to also detour through Web APIs first (mirroring `setTimeout`'s shape exactly: Call Stack → Web APIs → its queue → Call Stack), treating the existing `Promise.resolve().then(...)` code text as a deliberate stand-in for an async call like `fetch`. Did not rename the displayed source code to `fetch(...)` — not explicitly requested, and the existing code text + a distinct Web API status (`"Pending..."`, not reusing `setTimeout`'s `"Timer started"`) communicates the same lesson without changing the literal code shown in the panel.
    - Rewrote `scenarios.ts` from 8 frames to a fully event-loop-accurate **12 frames** (`TOTAL_STEPS` is just `EVENT_LOOP_FRAMES.length`, so "Step X of 12" updates with zero changes to the hook or any component): `setTimeout` now flows Call Stack (register) → Web APIs (`"Timer started"`) → Task Queue (`"Waiting..."`, queued the instant the timer fires, several frames before the Call Stack ever empties) → Call Stack (`"Executing..."`) → empty. `Promise.resolve().then(...)` now flows the identical shape — Call Stack (register) → Web APIs (`"Pending..."`) → Microtask Queue (`"Waiting..."`) → Call Stack (`"Executing..."`) → empty — rather than skipping Web APIs. Both queues sit populated simultaneously for several frames (steps 6–8) before either drains, and the Microtask Queue is fully emptied (step 9) before the Task Queue is touched at all (step 11) — the actual event-loop priority rule the whole feature exists to teach.
    - Re-verified the complete 12-frame sequence via direct DOM text inspection of all 4 panels at every single step (not a screenshot), with the browser's cache disabled on a fresh navigation. Hit the same false-read pattern as Follow-up 2 once more — a 500ms-per-step wait showed step 11's Task Queue one frame stale (still showing the dequeued item) — re-ran with 1000ms waits and got the exact authored state at every step, confirming it was the same render/exit-animation timing artifact, not a logic bug. Re-ran typecheck/lint clean after the rewrite.

Added a required `emptyLabel` prop to `SimulatorPanel`, with each wrapper passing contextual text ("Stack is empty", "No active requests", "Queue is empty" ×2).
    3. **Important** — no `aria-live` region anywhere, so a screen-reader user pressing Play/Step got zero announcement that anything changed. Added a `description` field to every `SimulatorFrame` (a plain-English sentence per step) and a `sr-only role="status" aria-live="polite"` span in `EventLoopSimulator.tsx` announcing `"Step X of Y: {description}"`.
    4. **Minor** — decorative elements lacked `aria-hidden`: `BrowserChromeBar`'s traffic-light dots + fake address text, and `CodePanel`'s gutter ping-dot. Added `aria-hidden="true"` to both.
    5. **Minor** — the live-indicator ping dots (`PanelHeader`, `CodePanel`) used raw Tailwind `animate-ping`, which doesn't respect `prefers-reduced-motion`. Added a guard so the ping span is omitted (leaving just the static dot) when reduced motion is preferred, in `PanelHeader`, `CodePanel`, and the new Call Stack pulse (next item).
    6. **Minor** — `ExecutionOrder`'s labels (`"Promise.then(...)"`/`"setTimeout(...)"`) didn't match what Call Stack actually showed while executing (`"console.log('promise')"`/`"console.log('timeout')"`). Aligned `EXECUTION_ORDER_BY_OUTPUT` in `scenarios.ts` to the executing-time labels.
    7. **Minor** (Layer 1 gap) — build-plan's "Active item in CallStack has pulse highlight" wasn't distinctly implemented — every panel's dot looked identical. Added a `pulse` prop to `SimulatorPanel` (only `CallStack` sets it), rendering the same ping-style dot already used elsewhere instead of a plain static one, for Call Stack items specifically.
    8. Two Layer 1 deviations from `architecture.md`'s literal documented shape (a single `frame` object instead of separate `callStack`/`webAPIs`/etc. state fields; `OutputPanel.tsx` instead of the documented `ConsoleOutput.tsx` name) were reviewed and left as-is — both are deliberate, reasonable design choices already covered by this entry's own documentation, not defects to fix.
    - **Real bug caught while fixing #5, not by the review itself:** calling Framer Motion's `useReducedMotion()` directly inside `PanelHeader`/`CodePanel`/`SimulatorPanel` (all part of the server-rendered tree) caused a genuine hydration mismatch — confirmed via a Playwright context with `reducedMotion: 'reduce'`, which threw a real React hydration-mismatch error and a "script tag" warning. Root cause: the hook can resolve the real media-query value synchronously on the client's very first render (before hydration completes), which doesn't match the server's unconditional `false`. This is the same root-cause shape already solved once in this codebase for `ThemeProvider` (see Decisions Made → 01 Design System) — fixed the same way: added `hooks/useSafeReducedMotion.ts` using `useSyncExternalStore` (`getServerSnapshot` always `false`, `getSnapshot` the real `window.matchMedia` check) instead of a `useState`+`useEffect` "has mounted" flag, which also avoids the `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` lint error a naive fix would have hit (confirmed by hitting that exact lint error on the first attempt before switching to `useSyncExternalStore`). Re-verified with the same reduced-motion Playwright context afterward: zero hydration errors, ping dots correctly absent, plain dots correctly present.
    - Also caught and fixed a performance slip introduced by the same fix: `useReducedMotion()`/`useSafeReducedMotion()` was initially called once per `CodeLine` (11 times per render, one per code line) instead of once in the parent `CodePanel` and passed down — fixed before it ever shipped.
    - Re-verified everything together: typecheck/lint clean; Playwright confirmed all 4 panels' empty states, the aria-live text per step, the Call Stack pulse (present normally, absent under reduced motion, with zero hydration errors), and the aligned Execution Order labels — both via direct DOM/computed-style checks and screenshots, desktop/mobile × light/dark; re-ran the full 12-step regression check with cache disabled and got byte-for-byte the same correct sequence as before these fixes. One dark-mode screenshot via a `page.reload()`-based test came back fully blank below the navbar despite correct DOM/colors/opacity underneath (confirmed via direct computed-style inspection) — re-tested with a single `page.goto()` + `addInitScript()` instead of a `reload()` and got a correct screenshot immediately, isolating it to a `reload()`-specific Playwright/dev-server timing quirk in this long-running session, not an application defect.

    1. **Initially skipped, then reversed by explicit user request.** CodeRabbit flagged `hidden md:block` on `SimulatorControls` as hiding all playback controls on mobile. First response: confirmed this was deliberate (it directly implemented `ui-rules.md`'s "Hero simulator is simplified (no interactive controls) on mobile" rule, decided during this feature's own architect session) and skipped it. User then explicitly asked to apply CodeRabbit's suggestion anyway — overriding the earlier architect-session decision for this feature specifically. Removed the `hidden md:block` wrapper entirely; `SimulatorControls`' existing `flex flex-wrap` layout handled the narrower width with no further changes needed, wrapping cleanly onto two rows (Play/Step Back/Step Forward/Restart, then Autoplay + Speed) on a 390px viewport. Verified interaction actually works on mobile (tapped Step forward, step advanced correctly) and confirmed no new overflow (`scrollWidth` 719 vs `clientWidth` 390 — within the already-documented `CompanyLogosStrip` fluctuation range). `ui-rules.md`'s general "simplified on mobile" guidance is unchanged for future simulators (10–12) — this is a confirmed, per-feature exception, not a revision to the general rule.
    2. **Fixed — real type-safety gap.** `ExecutionOrder`/`OutputPanel` typed `consoleOutput` as `string[]` and indexed `EXECUTION_ORDER_BY_OUTPUT[output]` without a defined-check — sound today only because `scenarios.ts`'s own data happens to be internally consistent, but nothing enforced that. Added `CONSOLE_OUTPUT_TOKENS`/`ConsoleOutputToken` (a closed `"start" | "end" | "promise" | "timeout"` union) to `types.ts`, retyped `SimulatorFrame.consoleOutput` and both components' props to use it, and retyped `EXECUTION_ORDER_BY_OUTPUT` as `Record<ConsoleOutputToken, ExecutionOrderEntry>` (exhaustive, not `Record<string, ...>`) — the lookup is now sound by construction; a typo'd or future invalid output token fails at compile time instead of risking a runtime crash.
    3. **Fixed — real UX bug.** `PanelHeader`'s "Running" badge was hardcoded green text regardless of actual play state — inherited unchanged from the original static mock, where it was just decorative flavor text since nothing was real yet. Now that the engine has genuine play/pause state (default: paused, frozen at step 1), the hardcoded label was actively wrong most of the time. Added an `isPlaying` prop; badge now reads "Paused" (`bg-surface-secondary text-text-secondary`, muted dot) when not playing and "Running" (existing green treatment) when playing.
    - Re-verified: typecheck/lint clean; Playwright confirmed the badge text and color swap correctly on Play/Pause (text assertions, not just a screenshot), and re-ran the full 12-step DOM regression to confirm the type changes didn't alter any runtime behavior — byte-for-byte the same correct sequence as before.
**Last completed:** 10 React Rendering Simulator (Hero Version) — ran the `architect` skill before building. Two decisions resolved with the user up front: (1) Feature 09's own notes flagged "revisit chrome extraction when a second simulator actually needs it" — this is that second simulator, so `BrowserChromeBar`/`PanelHeader`/`SimulatorControls`/`useSafeReducedMotion` were extracted out of `event-loop/` into `src/components/shared/simulator-chrome/` + `src/hooks/` (pure move, zero behavior change, `event-loop/`'s own copies deleted and its imports repointed) rather than duplicating them a second time; (2) render-vs-commit semantics — Render Phase highlights Counter, Display, **and** Button (React calls every child by default, no memoization), Diffing/DOM-Update narrow to only Display as actually changed/patched, matching real React behavior rather than a simplified "only Counter+Display ever highlight" story.
  - New self-contained feature module: `src/features/simulators/react-rendering/` — `types.ts`, `data/scenarios.ts` (`RENDER_FRAMES`, 5 frames), `hooks/useReactRenderingSimulator.ts`, `components/{ReactRenderingSimulator,ComponentTree,VirtualDomPanel,RenderFlowStepper}.tsx`. Imports nothing from `event-loop/`, only from the new shared `simulator-chrome`.
  - Scenario tree is fixed/hardcoded (`App → Counter → [Display, Button]`, state in `Counter`) — same "one hardcoded Hero-version scenario" scope as Event Loop. 5 stages (`click → state-update → render → diffing → dom-update`), deviating from build-plan's literal 5-stage wording (which lists "Virtual DOM" as its own timed step) to match `designs/hero-section-1-react-rendering.png`'s actual structure instead, where Virtual DOM is the live state panel, not a stage — same "design overrides literal build-plan text" precedent as `CompanyLogosStrip`/`ConceptSwitcherTabs`.
  - **Real decision surfaced during verification, not anticipated in the architect session:** `ui-rules.md` had a standing rule — "default to hiding controls on mobile for new simulators (10–12) unless asked otherwise," written when Event Loop's full-mobile-controls was still considered a one-off CodeRabbit-driven exception. Built this feature copying `EventLoopSimulator.tsx`'s structure directly (full controls visible, no mobile hide), which silently broke that standing rule without re-confirming it should carry over. Caught this during the verification pass (before declaring the feature done) and asked the user directly rather than silently picking a side — also flagged a wrinkle specific to this simulator: the **Increment** button lives inside `VirtualDomPanel` (part of the visualization itself, build-plan's literal trigger), not inside the generic `SimulatorControls` bar, so hiding just the bottom bar wouldn't actually freeze the simulator the way Event Loop's mobile-hide originally intended.   - **Follow-up 2 (real rearchitecture — "what's the meaning of simulator", same session, before any commit):** the v2 redesign (matching the design's content) still wasn't enough — the user's sharper, more fundamental feedback: the card row was narrower/shorter than `EventLoopSimulator`, the 5 cards weren't evenly distributed (dead space after the last card), the Render Phase spinner animated forever regardless of step, label/note text didn't align across cards, and — the real issue — every card's content was pre-filled and static; clicking through steps just moved a highlight ring over an already-finished diagram, with Virtual DOM/Diffing/Real DOM all showing their final answer from step 1. Correctly called this out as not being a simulation at all.
    - **Root cause of the even-distribution bug:** `flex-1` was applied to `StageCard`'s own root div, one nesting level too deep — it tried to grow relative to its immediate sibling (an arrow icon) inside a small per-card flex wrapper, but the 5 per-card wrapper divs themselves had no `flex-1` in the *outer* row, so they only took natural content width, leaving the row short of full width. Fixed structurally with a CSS grid (`grid-cols-[1fr_auto_1fr_auto_1fr_auto_1fr_auto_1fr]` — 5 equal-`1fr` card tracks + 4 `auto` arrow tracks) rather than re-tuning flex values, since a grid can't produce uneven columns the way nested flex did here.
    - **Real rearchitecture, not a visual tweak, for "nothing is happening":** redesigned `RenderFrame` from a single `activeCard` index to a 5-tuple `cardStatuses: CardStatuses` (`"pending" | "active" | "done"` per card) plus separate `componentStateCount`/`realDomCount` fields (these go stale at different times — internal state updates at step 2, the real DOM doesn't catch up until step 6, which is the whole point). Every stage body component now branches on its own status: `pending` renders a new shared `PendingPlaceholder` (faded icon + hourglass, "Waiting…") instead of the final content; `active`/`done` reveal the real visual with a one-time entrance animation (scale+fade for Virtual DOM/Diffing's trees, a scale-pop on Component State's and Real DOM's count when the value actually changes). Render Phase's spinner now only renders during its own `active` frame — `done` swaps it for a static `CheckCircle2`, so it stops for good instead of spinning forever. Real DOM is the deliberate exception to the placeholder pattern: it never shows a ghost, always showing its real (initially stale) count — `Count: 0` through steps 1–5, flipping to `Count: 1` only at step 6 — which is what actually demonstrates "the real DOM doesn't change until React commits," the concrete lesson the simulator exists to teach.
    - **Alignment fix:** `StageCard.tsx` now exports `CardZones`, a 3-row layout (fixed-height label row, flexible centered visual row, fixed-height note row) every stage-body renders into — guarantees every card's label/note sit at the same y-position regardless of how tall that card's own visual is.
    - **Sizing fix, measured not guessed:** captured `EventLoopSimulator`'s live `getBoundingClientRect()` (1216×718 at a 1440px viewport) as a concrete target before changing anything. The grid fix above already solved width for free; bumped `StageCard`'s `min-height` 260px→340px (`p-3`→`p-4`, `gap-2`→`gap-3`) to close the height gap, re-measured afterward: 1216×706 — width exact, height within ~12px.
    - File split: the 5 stage-body components moved from inline functions inside `StageCardsRow.tsx` to their own files under `components/stage-bodies/` — each now has real per-status branching and its own animation, no longer "small enough to inline."

One dark-mode screenshot caught a "Compiling…" badge mid-frame from a cold recompile right after editing `StageCard.tsx` — re-screenshotted after a warm reload and got the correct state, not a real bug.

**Last completed:** 11 Browser Pipeline Simulator (Hero Version) — ran the `architect` skill before building. The user explicitly rejected `designs/hero-section-1-browser-pipeline.png` (a flat row of 8 small icon cards) up front and asked for a creative redesign rather than a literal build. Proposed and got sign-off on a structural change before writing any code: real browsers parse HTML and CSS **in parallel**, not as one flat 8-step sequence — built as 2 parallel parse tracks (HTML→DOM, CSS→CSSOM) merging into one sequential pipeline (Render Tree → Layout → Paint → Composite), with a persistent 8-stage rail across the top so the build-plan's literal "all 8 stages light up" requirement stays visible even though the real visualization groups them into 2 rows. Also agreed up front on the scenario content: a small page with a `.ad { display: none }` rule, giving the Render Tree frame something concrete to visibly exclude — a real "why is the Render Tree smaller than the DOM" teaching moment, the same kind of specific-not-generic content this project has favored since Feature 06.
  - New self-contained feature module: `src/features/simulators/browser-pipeline/` — `types.ts`, `data/scenarios.ts` (`PIPELINE_FRAMES`, 7 frames), `hooks/useBrowserPipelineSimulator.ts`, `components/{BrowserPipelineSimulator,StageRail,ParseTracksRow,ParseTrack,TreeView,PipelineStagesRow,PipelineCard,RenderTreeVisual,LayoutVisual,PaintVisual,CompositeVisual,PageMockupContent,PendingPlaceholder}.tsx`. Imports nothing from `event-loop/`/`react-rendering/`, only the shared `simulator-chrome` — same self-containment invariant as Features 09–10.
  - **Real design decision, matching the established invariant:** `PIPELINE_FRAMES` frames are complete authored snapshots (`stageStatuses: Record<StageId, StageStatus>` per frame) — the hook just indexes in, never derives a frame at runtime. HTML/CSS parsing deliberately share frames 2–3 (both tracks `"active"`/`"done"` together) to represent that they run concurrently rather than as separate sequential steps, which is the whole point of the parallel-tracks structural decision.
  - **Output visual, satisfied without a bolted-on extra component:** the build-plan's "output visual at the end" requirement is met by the Composite frame itself — `PaintVisual`/`CompositeVisual` share one `PageMockupContent` component (the same 4 real styled boxes: bold header, h1, teal intro line, gray footer), with `PaintVisual` passing a `layered` prop (each box offset 2–8px via `translate-x-*`/`-translate-y-*` + `shadow-sm`, suggesting un-composited separate layers) and `CompositeVisual` rendering the identical content flat — the final visual the user sees, visibly missing the ad banner the Render Tree frame excluded earlier in the walkthrough.
  - **Two measurement false-alarms caught and correctly ruled out during verification, not real bugs (documented in `ui-registry.md` for reuse):** (1) `getComputedStyle(el).transform` returned `"none"` for every `translate-x-*`/`-translate-y-*` element, including the already-shipped `PlatformGraph`'s centering transform used as a sanity check — root cause is that **Tailwind v4 implements `translate`/`scale`/`rotate` via the individual CSS properties of the same name, not the legacy `transform` property**, so `.transform` will never reflect them in this project; confirmed correct via `getComputedStyle(el).translate` instead (`"2px -2px"`, `"4px -4px"`, etc., matching the intended per-box stagger exactly) and matching `getBoundingClientRect()` offsets. (2) A `StageRail` connector-color check first reported `found: false` — an escaped-CSS-selector mistake in the test script itself, not a missing element; re-queried via `className.includes(...)` and confirmed the connectors render the correct `bg-info`/`bg-premium` colors once each track stage is `"done"`. Neither required a code change.

  - Verified: typecheck (`npx tsc --noEmit`) and lint (`npx eslint`) clean. Playwright: stepped through all 7 frames confirming `aria-live` text and DOM state match `scenarios.ts` exactly, zero console/page errors; full viewport matrix (desktop 1440, laptop 1366×768, tablet portrait 768×1024, tablet landscape 1024×768, mobile 390×844, × light/dark where applicable) all console-clean with **zero** horizontal overflow at every width (`scrollWidth === clientWidth` exactly, including 390/390 on mobile — better than the pre-existing `CompanyLogosStrip` baseline slack); reduced-motion Playwright context clean; confirmed switching Browser Pipeline → Event Loop → React Rendering and back still renders each simulator's real content (switcher integrity unbroken by the new tab).
  - **Follow-up (tree connector + build-animation rework, same session, before any PR):** user reviewed screenshots and said the trees "aren't properly built" — every child at a level hung off one shared vertical tick with no per-child connector (reading as a floating row under a single line, not a branching tree), and a revealed level appeared as one instant block of text rather than node-by-node. Rebuilt `TreeView.tsx` to fix both: (1) a real pure-CSS org-chart connector — each child wrapper renders its own short horizontal half-bar (transparent on the outer edge for the first/last child, colored otherwise) plus its own vertical drop, so N children under one parent produce one continuous horizontal junction with N individual drops, not a single shared line (no SVG, no `getBoundingClientRect()` measurement needed — the classic CSS-only tree-diagram technique); (2) every node is now a `motion.span` with `transition.delay = depth * 0.3 + siblingIndex * 0.12` — since a node only renders once its depth is within `revealDepth`, going from not-rendered to rendered is a genuine fresh mount each time a level is newly revealed, so the stagger plays automatically with no replay logic. `prefersReducedMotion` is computed once in each caller (`ParseTrack`/`RenderTreeVisual`) and threaded down as a prop, not called per recursive node — same "hoist the hook" fix this project already made once for `CodePanel` in Feature 09.
  - Re-verified after the rework: typecheck/lint clean; full 7-frame regression byte-for-byte the same correct `aria-live` text as before (confirms this was a presentation-only change); zero console errors including under reduced motion; directly confirmed via `getComputedStyle().opacity` (not a screenshot) that a depth-2 node sits at `0` shortly after a fresh reveal while the depth-0 root is already `1`, proving the build genuinely plays node-by-node; screenshots confirm the DOM/CSSOM/Render Tree cards now read as real branching org-charts with the excluded `aside.ad` node's connector visibly intact.
  - **Follow-up (`/review`, manual pass against 4 inline review comments, same session, before any commit):** ran the project's `review` skill manually (project-local skills aren't registered with the Skill tool) against 4 CodeRabbit-style inline findings on `PipelineStagesRow.tsx`/`StageRail.tsx`'s mask-image and `TreeView.tsx`'s motion props. Verified each against the actual current code rather than acting on the finding text alone, per this project's standing CodeRabbit-findings rule — all 4 turned out to be false positives, zero code changes made:
    1. **Mask-image "hardcoded black" (2 findings, `PipelineStagesRow`/`StageRail`).** The suggested fix referenced "the shared tokenized mask utility class... already used elsewhere" — grepped the codebase and confirmed no such utility exists; `react-rendering/StageCardsRow.tsx` and `CompanyLogosStrip.tsx` both inline the identical `[mask-image:linear-gradient(to_right,transparent,black_*%,black_*%,transparent)]` arbitrary value independently, and no `--color-mask`/`--mask` token exists in `globals.css`. Also substantively wrong on the rule itself: `black`/`transparent` in a `mask-image` gradient are alpha-channel opacity stops, not rendered hues — `AGENTS.md`'s "no hardcoded colors" rule (`bg-teal-600`/`text-gray-700`/`bg-[#hex]` examples) targets visible UI color, which this isn't; any fully-opaque keyword would mask identically. Skipped — copying the exact already-shipped, already-reviewed pattern is correct here, not a regression.
    2. **`TreeView.tsx` missing `layout` prop.** `ui-rules.md`'s rule ("items moving between panels get `layout`") is for elements that move between distinct containers — confirmed via the only real usage in this codebase, `event-loop/SimulatorPanel.tsx`'s `layoutId` for items moving between Call Stack/Web APIs/queues. A tree node never moves between containers, it fades/scales in once in place; none of the other "reveal once" components in this project (`ComponentStateBody`, `VirtualDomBody`, etc.) use `layout` either. Adding it would risk an unwanted FLIP-slide when siblings are added mid-stagger — the opposite of the entrance just built. Skipped.
    3. **`TreeView.tsx` missing `AnimatePresence` wrapper.** The `motion.span` has no `exit` prop — wrapping it in `AnimatePresence` without one is a no-op (`AnimatePresence` only animates removal if the child defines `exit`). Checked where this pattern is used for real (`ExecutionOrder`/`OutputPanel`/`SimulatorPanel`, always paired with an explicit `exit={{...}}`) and confirmed none of the other simulators' "reveal once" components animate the un-reveal direction (stepping back just instantly swaps to a placeholder) — this would be new scope, not a bug fix. Skipped.

Root cause: this machine's network path presents its own certificate instead of Supabase's real one when connecting to the pooler (local TLS interception — confirmed Supabase itself was reachable via a plain `Test-NetConnection` TCP check first, ruling out a network-reachability problem before chasing a TLS one). Fixed with explicit, scoped exceptions — not a blanket disable: `drizzle.config.ts`'s `dbCredentials` strips `sslmode=` from the URL (it was overriding the separate `ssl` option otherwise) and sets `ssl: { rejectUnauthorized: false }` (CLI tooling only, never runs in production); `src/lib/db.ts` sets `ssl: process.env.NODE_ENV === "production" ? true : { rejectUnauthorized: false }` so Vercel/production keeps full certificate verification and only this affected local dev machine relaxes it. Got explicit user sign-off before running anything with verification relaxed, since it's a real security trade-off (weaker protection against a MITM on whatever local path is intercepting) — confirmed reasonable given the cause is local-network-specific, not a Supabase-side issue.

  - **Real architectural change, not scope creep:** Next has no per-route opt-out for a root layout, so honoring decision (1) required restructuring `src/app/layout.tsx` into a bare shell (`ThemeProvider`/`TooltipProvider` only) plus a new `src/app/(main)/layout.tsx` owning `Navbar`/`Footer`. `src/app/page.tsx` (the homepage) moved to `src/app/(main)/page.tsx` unchanged. Verified via Playwright that the homepage renders byte-for-byte the same after the move (same Navbar, same sections, zero console errors) — this was a pure structural move, not a rewrite.
  - **Real infrastructure bug found during end-to-end verification, not assumed fixed:** clicking either OAuth button returned a 500 from `/api/auth/sign-in/social`. Server log showed `SELF_SIGNED_CERT_IN_CHAIN` on the `verification` table insert — the same TLS-interception issue already documented for this machine (see Feature 14 notes), but never actually exercised by a DB **write** until this feature's first real sign-in attempt (`/api/auth/ok` is a static health check with no DB query at all, so it never proved writes worked). Root cause confirmed via a standalone `pg.Pool` repro outside Next/Turbopack: this `pg` version now aliases `sslmode=require` in the connection string to `verify-full`, which silently overrides the existing app-level `ssl: { rejectUnauthorized: false }` in `db.ts`. Fix (confirmed working via the same repro before touching the real file): appended `&uselibpqcompat=true` to `DATABASE_URL` in `.env.local` — `pg`'s own deprecation warning names this exact flag to restore the originally-intended "encrypt, don't verify" behavior. Local-only env change (`.env.local` is gitignored); `db.ts`/`drizzle.config.ts` code is untouched and still correct.
  - **Follow-up (`/review`, same session, before any PR):** ran the project's `review` skill manually against the finished feature. Found 4 issues (1 critical, 1 important, 2 minor), all fixed:
    1. **Critical — the error banner could never actually fire.** `OAuthButton` called `signIn.social()` without `errorCallbackURL`. Traced Better-Auth's actual default in `oauth2/state.mjs`: with no `errorCallbackURL`, failures redirect to `${baseURL}/error` — a route that doesn't exist in this app — never to `/login?error=...`. Fixed by passing `errorCallbackURL: "/login"` explicitly. Re-verified end-to-end via raw `curl` against `/api/auth/sign-in/social` → `/api/auth/callback/google?error=access_denied` with a real session cookie: confirmed `Location: /login?error=access_denied`, and a Playwright check confirmed the page renders the exact mapped copy ("Sign-in was cancelled. Try again whenever you're ready.").
    2. **Important — no handling of a non-throwing failure.** `authClient.signIn.social()` returns `{ data, error }` rather than always redirecting; `handleClick` never checked `error`, so a misconfigured/down provider would leave the button permanently disabled with a spinner and no feedback. Fixed: on `error`, resets `isPending` and navigates to `/login?error=<code>` itself, reusing the same `getLoginErrorMessage` mapping (single source of truth for error copy).
    3. **Minor — inconsistent icon pattern.** `GithubIcon` followed the established `XLogo` precedent (`cn("size-4", className)`, `aria-hidden`); `GoogleIcon` had neither. Aligned.
    4. Noted, not a defect: the layout restructure and the `.env.local` SSL fix above were unplanned-but-necessary — both required to deliver the agreed decisions, not scope creep.

**Last completed:** 16 Profile Provisioning Hook — ran the `architect` skill before building. The sequencing conflict this file's own previous "Next" note flagged was real: this feature's hook needs `profiles`, which is Feature 18's table. Resolved with the user up front rather than discovered mid-build: pull only the `profiles` table forward now (it's self-contained — nothing in Phase 3 references it, only the other direction), not the other 10 Feature 18 tables — same "pull forward just the needed mechanism" precedent as Feature 10 absorbing Feature 13's tab-switching. Two more decisions resolved before writing code: (1) `profiles.id` is a Postgres `text` column, not literal `uuid` as `architecture.md` previously implied — it must match `user.id`'s actual column type for the FK to validate, same pattern already used by `session.userId`/`account.userId`; (2) since OAuth never supplies a username (`profiles.username` is `NOT NULL UNIQUE` and no feature in any of the 39 lets a user set one), generate it by slugifying the OAuth display name, retrying with a random suffix on a `23505` collision, falling back to a fully random slug after a few attempts; (3) a failure inside the hook is caught and logged, never rethrown — the Better-Auth `user` row is already committed by the time `after` runs, so blocking sign-in over a profile-write failure would strand the account in a worse, unrecoverable state.
  - New files: `src/lib/schema/profiles.ts` (Drizzle table + `profilesRelations`), `src/lib/auth/provisionProfile.ts` (the slugify → insert-with-retry → `onConflictDoUpdate(id)` logic, exported standalone so it could be exercised directly without a real OAuth round-trip). `src/lib/schema/index.ts` re-exports the new table. `src/lib/auth/server.ts`'s `databaseHooks.user.create.after` replaced its Feature 14 stub with a try/catch around `provisionProfile`.
  - Ran `npx drizzle-kit generate` + `npx drizzle-kit migrate` to create `profiles` against the live Supabase Postgres instance — confirmed via `information_schema.columns` that all 14 columns landed with the right types.
  - **Real pre-existing bug found and fixed, not assumed away — `drizzle-kit migrate` failed outright (not the "hangs forever" TLS symptom documented for Feature 14).** `drizzle.config.ts`'s `sslmode` stripping used a regex (`.replace(/[?&]sslmode=[^&]+/, "")`) that only correctly handled `sslmode` as the *last* query param. The live `DATABASE_URL` is `...postgres?sslmode=require&uselibpqcompat=true` — stripping `?sslmode=require` left a dangling `&uselibpqcompat=true` with no leading `?`, which `pg`'s URL parser folded into the database name (`"postgres&uselibpqcompat=true" does not exist`). Confirmed via a standalone `pg.Client` script before touching the real file (same diagnostic method as Feature 14's TLS bug). Fixed by parsing with `URL`/`URLSearchParams.delete("sslmode")` instead of a regex — correct regardless of `sslmode`'s position or what follows it. Also confirmed via the same script that `src/lib/db.ts`'s approach (explicit `ssl` option, no stripping) was never actually affected — the explicit `ssl` config does take precedence over `sslmode` in the connection string for a raw `pg.Client`/`Pool`; only `drizzle-kit`'s own regex was broken.
  - **Second real bug caught by direct verification, not by inspection:** the first version of `isUsernameConflict()` checked `error.code`/`error.constraint` on the caught error directly. Drizzle wraps the raw `pg` error in a `DrizzleQueryError` — the fields it needed are on `error.cause`, not on the thrown error itself — so every real username collision was falling through to the unhandled `throw error` branch instead of triggering a retry. Caught by exercising `provisionProfile()` directly against the live DB with two synthetic same-named users (via a temporary script, deleted before commit) rather than trusting the code by reading it; fixed by unwrapping `error.cause` before checking `.code`/`.constraint`. Re-ran the same script afterward and confirmed all 3 scenarios work: a fresh insert, a same-name collision correctly retried with a random suffix, and a same-`id` re-invocation correctly hitting `onConflictDoUpdate` (refreshes `fullName`/`email`/`avatarUrl`/`updatedAt` only — `username`/`xp`/`streak`/`isPremium` untouched).
  - **Verification method, and why no live click-through OAuth test was done:** real Google/GitHub sign-in requires completing third-party login UI (credentials, possibly 2FA) that can't be automated here — Feature 15's "end-to-end" OAuth check only confirmed the redirect to the provider's consent screen, not a completed round-trip. Verified the hook's actual logic directly instead: typecheck/lint clean, then exercised `provisionProfile()` against the live database with real `user` rows backing the FK (Postgres correctly rejected a non-existent `user.id` with a `23503` foreign-key violation on the first attempt — confirming the constraint itself works — before switching to real inserted `user` rows for the 3 scenarios above).
  - **Real data gap found and backfilled while verifying:** querying the live `user` table during verification surfaced one real row — an actual Google sign-in from earlier in this session (before this feature existed), with no matching `profiles` row, since the old stub hook never ran any logic. Ran `provisionProfile()` once for that real user (not a synthetic one) to bring the dataset in line with what this feature guarantees going forward; confirmed the resulting row (`username: "superpower-india"`, correct `avatarUrl` from the Google profile image).
  - Updated `architecture.md` (the `profiles` table's "pulled forward" note + corrected `id` type; the `databaseHooks` example now shows the real try/catch) and `library-docs.md` (matching example update + a new "Username generation" pattern note, including the `error.cause` unwrapping gotcha) so a future session reading either doesn't see the old Feature-16-is-stubbed framing.
  - **Follow-up (`/review`, then CodeRabbit findings, same session, before merge):** ran the project's `review` skill manually against the finished feature. Found 1 minor issue (the `after` hook's `console.error` was missing the `[tag]` prefix convention every other server-side log line in this codebase uses). Before that fix landed, CodeRabbit flagged a related but more serious issue on the open PR: that same `console.error` call logged the raw `error` object, which — verified directly, not assumed — is a real PII leak: Drizzle's `DrizzleQueryError` embeds the full insert payload (`fullName`/`email`/`avatarUrl`) directly in its message. CodeRabbit also suggested dropping `user.id` from the log; checked that against `error-handling.md`'s own logging strategy first and found it explicitly sanctions logging `userId` (its rate-limit example) — that part of the finding didn't hold, so `user.id` stayed. Fixed by adding `getProfileProvisioningErrorCode()` to `provisionProfile.ts`, which extracts only the Postgres error code (e.g. `"23505"`) from `error.cause` — never the raw error/message/params — and folded in the missing `[auth]` tag from the `/review` finding at the same time. Re-verified directly against the live DB afterward that the username-collision retry path still works unchanged after the refactor. Pushed as a second commit (`04c06ae`) on the same branch/PR.
  - **Investigated, not a defect in this feature:** while verifying, found two real `BetterAuthError: State mismatch` entries in the dev server's own log from earlier OAuth testing (predating this feature). Root-caused to this machine's tendency to leave a stray `next dev` process holding port 3000 — Better-Auth's OAuth state is pinned to the exact `BETTER_AUTH_URL` port with a 5-minute window, so a mid-flow port flip between sign-in and callback produces exactly this error. Confirmed not currently reproducing (single healthy dev-server process, full `/api/auth/sign-in/social` pipeline re-verified working). Documented as a durable environment gotcha in the cross-session memory system, not here — check there first if it recurs.

Center: search trigger button with ⌘K kbd chip. Mobile: hamburger menu with icon + label nav links; mobile right cluster mirrors the desktop gate. Streak/isPremium are placeholder values (0/false) — real profile data deferred to Feature 27.
  - **`fill-streak stroke-none` for solid SVG fills:** Tailwind v4 auto-generates `fill-*`/`stroke-*` utilities from custom CSS variables the same way `bg-*`/`text-*` already work — confirmed in Feature 06's `PlatformGraph`. `fill-streak` fills the flame solid orange; `stroke-none` prevents any inherited stroke from the icon's path. Use this pattern for any lucide icon intended as a solid fill rather than a stroked outline.
