    2. **`Navbar`: Flash of Log In button on refresh.** While the session was resolving (`isLoading: true`), `user` was null, so the full logged-out cluster (Follow on X + ThemeToggle + Log In) rendered briefly then swapped to the logged-in cluster. Root cause: the entire right cluster was inside a single `user ? ... : ...` branch, so every element changed at once. Fix: Follow on X and ThemeToggle are now rendered unconditionally; only the third slot (`{!isLoading && (user ? <UserDropdown /> : <Link>Log In</Link>)}`) changes — the stable anchor elements never flash, and the swap from nothing → UserDropdown is far less jarring than the full cluster swap.
    3. **`AppNavbar`: Items too crowded and ⌘K misaligned.** Container gap raised to `gap-5`; nav links got `ml-10` (extra separation from the logo) + `gap-2` between items; right cluster gap raised to `gap-5` with `shrink-0` added. For the ⌘K kbd: the `⌘` Unicode character has different vertical font metrics than `K` in monospace, causing misalignment that neither `leading-none` nor `inline-flex items-center` could fix at the text-node level. Replaced `⌘` with lucide-react's `Command` icon (`size-2.5`, SVG) — a proper glyph that sits exactly level with the `K` span regardless of font.
  - **Follow-up 2 (loading delay + sign-out UX, same session, before any PR):** two more defects found via continued live testing:
    1. **Login button and profile picture slow to appear (both logged-out and logged-in).** Root cause: `authClient.useSession()` is a client-side-only hook — it fires a network request to `/api/auth/get-session` on every page load. Until that round-trip completes, `isLoading` is `true` and the auth-dependent slot renders nothing, causing a visible ~100–500ms pop-in. Fix: call `auth.api.getSession({ headers: await headers() })` in the server layouts (`(main)/layout.tsx` and `(app)/layout.tsx`), which reads the session cookie directly from the request with zero network round-trip, and pass the result as `initialUser` down to both navbars. `useUser()` now accepts an optional `initialUser?: SessionUser | null` parameter — when provided (even as `null` for logged-out), `isLoading` is always `false` from the very first render, so the correct state paints immediately with no pop-in. `SessionUser` type exported from `useUser.ts`. Both layouts are now `async` Server Components. A `try/catch` around `auth.api.getSession()` ensures a DB error falls back to client-side loading (the original behavior) without crashing the page.
    2. **Sign-out button: icon invisible on hover; no visible feedback after clicking.** Two separate bugs: (a) `DropdownMenuItem`'s base class includes `not-data-[variant=destructive]:focus:**:text-accent-foreground`, which on hover forces ALL descendant SVGs to `accent-foreground` color, overriding the icon's inherited `text-error` and making it disappear against the `bg-error-muted` hover background. Fixed by using `variant="destructive"` on the item (which exempts it from that rule) and overriding the resulting destructive-color classes with the project's own `text-error`/`focus:bg-error-muted`/`focus:text-error` tokens, plus `[&_svg]:!text-error` to pin the icon's color with `!important` against any remaining descendant selector. (b) The `Loader2` spinner was placed *inside* the menu item, but `@base-ui/react/menu` closes the popup the instant you click any item — before React re-renders with `isSigningOut: true`, so the spinner was never visible. Fixed by moving the loading state to the `DropdownMenuTrigger` (the avatar button) itself: when `isSigningOut` is true, the avatar fades to 30% opacity, a `Loader2 animate-spin` appears centered over it, and the trigger is `disabled` so the menu can't be reopened while the sign-out request is in flight. The avatar is always visible in the navbar even after the dropdown closes, so the feedback is seen immediately and lasts until `router.push("/")` navigates away.
  - **Follow-up (security hardening, after Feature 17 push, before PR):** addressed 5 security/quality gaps found during a post-push review of the auth feature as a whole — none were regressions introduced by Feature 17, all were pre-existing gaps now closed before the PR goes out:
    1. **Login redirect guard** — `src/app/(auth)/login/page.tsx` is now an `async` Server Component that calls `auth.api.getSession()` in a try/catch and redirects logged-in users to `/` before rendering. Deferred from Feature 15 → Feature 17 scope; now done. Redirect target is `/` (homepage) by product decision — `/learn` is still a stub.
    2. **`src/lib/env.ts` created** — typed wrapper for all env vars (`databaseUrl`, `betterAuthSecret`, `betterAuthUrl`, `googleClientId/Secret`, `githubClientId/Secret`, `upstashRedisUrl/Token`). Has `import "server-only"` at the top, which causes a build-time error if any Client Component ever accidentally imports it — secrets can't leak into the client bundle. `code-standards.md` always documented this file; it was never actually created until now.
    3. **`src/lib/db.ts` and `src/lib/auth/server.ts` updated** — both now import `env` from `@/lib/env` and use `env.databaseUrl` / `env.googleClientId` / etc. instead of raw `process.env.*`. The explicit `if (!process.env.DATABASE_URL)` startup guard in `db.ts` was removed (ownership moved to `env.ts`).
    4. **`src/lib/auth/client.ts` — added `"use client"` directive** — `createAuthClient()` uses browser APIs; without the directive a Server Component importing it would crash at runtime with no clear error. Now fails at build time instead.
    5. **HTTP security headers added to `next.config.ts`** — all 6 headers from `security.md` now ship on every response: `X-DNS-Prefetch-Control`, `X-Frame-Options: SAMEORIGIN` (clickjacking), `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` (MIME sniffing), `Referrer-Policy`, `Permissions-Policy`, `Content-Security-Policy`. Also restored conditional `allowedDevOrigins` via `NEXT_ALLOWED_DEV_ORIGINS` env var (was manually reverted to a hardcoded IP locally). **Real bug hit during implementation:** the initial CSP used `script-src 'self' 'unsafe-eval'` without `'unsafe-inline'`, which blocked Next.js's inline hydration scripts — all sections using Framer Motion's `whileInView` (which starts at `opacity: 0`) became permanently invisible because React never hydrated. Fixed by adding `'unsafe-inline'` to `script-src`. The proper long-term fix is nonce-based CSP via `proxy.ts`; noted in a comment in `next.config.ts`. `frame-src 'none'` is correct for now and must be updated to `'self'` when the code-execution sandbox iframe is added (Features 24–25).
    - `context/architecture.md` updated: `env.ts` added to the `lib/` folder listing; `Auth + DB Client Patterns` code examples updated to show `env.googleClientId/Secret` and `env.databaseUrl` instead of the old raw `process.env.*` calls; `client.ts` note updated to mention `"use client"` directive.
**Last completed:** 18 Database Tables — ran the `architect` skill before building. Six decisions resolved with the user up front: (1) `user_id` FK type is `text` (not `uuid`) on all user-owned tables — must match `profiles.id`'s physical Postgres column type for FK validation; (2) schema files split into two grouped files (`content.ts`: concepts, challenges, interview_questions, roadmaps, roadmap_steps; `user-data.ts`: user_concept_progress, xp_events, user_challenge_submissions, user_interview_reviews, bookmarks) rather than 10 individual files, mirroring the meaningful content/user-activity boundary; (3) seeding via a separate re-runnable `scripts/seed.ts` (not baked into migrations); (4) Drizzle relations defined for all 10 tables now, not deferred; (5) unique constraints added on `(user_id, concept_id)` for `user_concept_progress`, `(user_id, question_id)` for `user_interview_reviews`, and three partial unique indexes on `bookmarks` (one per entity type, with `WHERE col IS NOT NULL`); (6) all new timestamps use `{ withTimezone: true }` (architecture.md's `timestamptz`) — the existing `profiles.ts` timestamp columns were also migrated to `timestamptz` at the same time (safe: `premium_expires_at` was NULL for all users, `created_at`/`updated_at` values are UTC and convert cleanly).
  - New files: `src/lib/schema/content.ts`, `src/lib/schema/user-data.ts`, `scripts/seed.ts`. Updated: `src/lib/schema/profiles.ts` (timestamp fix + `many()` back-references to user-data tables), `src/lib/schema/index.ts` (re-exports both new files).
  - Migration `drizzle/0003_lively_swordsman.sql` generated and applied — 10 new tables created, `profiles` timestamps altered. Verified via `information_schema.tables`: all 10 tables present.
  - Seed results (confirmed via direct `COUNT(*)` queries): 8 concepts (one per category: javascript-runtime/browser-internals/react/css/typescript/accessibility/performance/system-design), 3 challenges (debounce/virtual-list/specificity-calculator), 20 interview questions (5 per collection across ff-75/ff-javascript/ff-react/ff-system-design), 2 roadmaps + 8 roadmap steps. All seed content is original, specific to real platform concepts, and re-runnable via `onConflictDoNothing()`.
  - Circular import pattern: `user-data.ts` imports from `profiles.ts`; `profiles.ts` imports from `user-data.ts`. Safe because Drizzle's `relations()` callbacks are arrow functions called lazily (after all modules are fully loaded), not at import time — the standard Drizzle cross-file relations pattern.
  - **Follow-up (`/review`, then fixed all 4 findings, same session, before any PR):** ran the project's `review` skill against the finished feature. Found 4 issues, all fixed:
    1. **Important — seed not idempotent for `interview_questions`**: `onConflictDoNothing()` was called without a target, and the table had no unique constraint beyond the UUID PK — so duplicate questions would be inserted on every re-run. Fixed by: (a) adding `unique("interview_questions_collection_order_unique").on(table.collection, table.orderIndex)` to the table definition in `content.ts`; (b) changing the seed's insert to `onConflictDoNothing({ target: [interviewQuestions.collection, interviewQuestions.orderIndex] })`. Re-run confirmed 0 new questions on the second pass.
    2. **Important — no CHECK constraints**: all `text` columns acting as enums (`category`, `difficulty`, `collection`, `event_type`, `status`) had no DB-level validation — any out-of-range string would insert silently. Fixed by adding `check()` constraints to both schema files using `sql.join(ARRAY.map(v => sql.raw(\`'${v}'\`)), sql\`, \`)` to build `IN (...)` clauses dynamically from the typed constant arrays — so the SQL values stay in sync with the TypeScript types automatically. Migration `drizzle/0004_quiet_rafael_vega.sql` generated and applied (8 CHECK constraints + 1 UNIQUE).
    3. **Important — no shared constants file**: enum values were repeated as raw strings in the schema and seed, with no single source of truth. Created `src/lib/constants.ts` exporting typed `as const` arrays and union types for: `CONCEPT_TABS`, `CONCEPT_CATEGORIES`, `CONCEPT_DIFFICULTIES`, `CHALLENGE_DIFFICULTIES`, `INTERVIEW_COLLECTIONS`, `XP_EVENT_TYPES`, `CHALLENGE_STATUSES`. Schema files and seed now import from this file — one place to change, everything stays in sync.
    4. **Minor — seed used `as const` on data arrays, causing type-assertion workarounds**: `CONCEPTS`, `CHALLENGES`, `INTERVIEW_QUESTIONS`, `ROADMAPS` were declared `as const`, making every nested array readonly. The insert types expected mutable arrays, leading to a `[...ch.testCases]` spread cast — a disguised type assertion. Fixed by removing all `as const` from the seed arrays and replacing them with explicit type annotations (`ConceptSeed[]`, `ChallengeSeed[]`, etc.) — TypeScript infers the literal types from the definitions without needing casts, and all 22+ `difficulty: "easy" as const` suffixes were stripped as redundant. Typecheck (`npx tsc --noEmit`) clean after the fix.
  - **Follow-up (CodeRabbit PR review findings, same session, after PR):** verified all 9 findings against current code; fixed 5, skipped 4:
    - Fixed: (1) `roadmap_steps` — added `unique("roadmap_steps_roadmap_order_unique").on(roadmapId, orderIndex)` to schema; migration `drizzle/0005_nosy_dormammu.sql` generated and applied (8 duplicate rows from pre-constraint seed runs were deduplicated before the ALTER); (2) `bookmarks` — added `bookmarks_exactly_one_target_check` CHECK enforcing exactly one of `concept_id`/`question_id`/`challenge_id` is non-null; (3) seed — `roadmapSteps` insert now uses `onConflictDoNothing({ target: [roadmapSteps.roadmapId, roadmapSteps.orderIndex] })` with an explicit target; (4) `UserDropdown` sign-out — timeout path now resets `isSigningOut` so the user can retry, rather than navigating away; (5) `useUser` — `initialUser` fallback is now only used while `isPending`; after session resolves, returns `null` if no live session (prevents stale-after-logout auth UI).
    - Skipped: (a) migration USING clauses — already applied, data was correct; (b) mojibake in seed.ts — confirmed only in comment decorators (`// ───`), not in seed data strings; (c) `DevAbortSuppressor` location — `components/providers/` is correct (same as `ThemeProvider`); (d) circular `profiles` ↔ `user-data` import — safe Drizzle pattern; all cross-references are inside lazy `relations()` callbacks and `.references(() => ...)` thunks, never accessed synchronously at module load time; no TDZ error has ever occurred.
  - Pushed `feature/18-database-tables` to origin; PR #30 → `develop` merged (merge commit `7bdaae1`). Local `develop` fast-forwarded to match.
**Last completed:** 19 Upstash Redis Setup — ran the `architect` skill (manually) before building. Resolved 3 decisions up front: (1) `lib/upstash.ts` exports both `redis` (`new Redis({ url: env.upstashRedisUrl, token: env.upstashRedisToken })`) and `ratelimit` (`slidingWindow(20, '10 s')`, `analytics: true`) — two exports so Better-Auth can consume the raw client while app routes call `ratelimit.limit()` directly, each hitting the same underlying Redis instance; (2) Better-Auth's deferred auth rate limiting from Feature 14 included in this feature — wired `secondaryStorage` (Upstash adapter with `get`/`set`/`delete`) + `rateLimit: { enabled: true, window: 60, max: 5, storage: "secondary-storage" }` matching `security.md`'s `/api/auth/*: 5 req / 60s` table, plus `session.storeSessionInDatabase: true` to keep sessions in Postgres as before rather than migrating to Redis-only; (3) created a minimal stub at `src/app/api/progress/route.ts` (POST: auth check → rate limit check → 501 Not Implemented) so the limiter has a real surface to test against rather than shipping untested infrastructure.
  - New files: `src/lib/upstash.ts` (exports `redis` + `ratelimit`), `src/app/api/progress/route.ts` (stub — auth + rate limit + 501). Modified: `src/lib/auth/server.ts` (added `secondaryStorage`, `rateLimit`, `session.storeSessionInDatabase: true`).
  - All Upstash packages (`@upstash/ratelimit ^2.0.8`, `@upstash/redis ^1.38.0`) and env vars (`env.upstashRedisUrl`/`env.upstashRedisToken` in `lib/env.ts`, CSP `connect-src https://*.upstash.io` in `next.config.ts`) were already in place from prior features — no new dependencies or config changes needed.
  - Verified end-to-end: 22 parallel authenticated requests to `POST /api/progress` — 20 returned 501 (passed rate limit), 2 returned 429 (blocked). Rate limiter confirmed working against live Upstash instance.
  - **Follow-up (`/review`, then fixed both findings, same session, before any PR):** ran the project's `review` skill against the finished feature. Layer 1 and Layer 3 passed clean. Layer 2 found 2 issues, both fixed:
    1. **Important** — `Redis.fromEnv()` in `lib/upstash.ts` bypassed `lib/env.ts`, violating `code-standards.md`'s explicit rule ("Never access `process.env` directly in feature code — always import from `lib/env.ts`"). `env.upstashRedisUrl`/`env.upstashRedisToken` were already typed in `lib/env.ts` for exactly this purpose but were unused. Fixed by replacing `Redis.fromEnv()` with `new Redis({ url: env.upstashRedisUrl, token: env.upstashRedisToken })`.
    2. **Minor** — `app/api/progress/route.ts` used `await headers()` from `next/headers` for the session check instead of `req.headers`, deviating from the pattern both `security.md` and `code-standards.md` establish. Left `req: Request` declared but unused. Fixed by switching to `req.headers` and removing the `next/headers` import.
    - Re-verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean after both fixes.

Premium `<Lock>` icon remains on the right.

**Last completed:** 22 Understand Tab — ran the `architect` skill before building. Four decisions resolved with the user, all the recommended option: (1) author **Event Loop only** as the reference MDX + a "guide coming soon" empty state for unauthored concepts (authoring 8 guides is content work, not engineering); (2) implement a **minimal real `/api/progress`** understand-write now (Feature 27 layers XP/streak/bonus on top); (3) the 4 info cards come from **MDX frontmatter** fields; (4) the "Mark as understood" button is **visible logged-out and redirects to `/login`** on click.

Resolved with the same children-as-props pattern as the header/rail: `page.tsx` builds `<UnderstandTab>` on the server and passes it as an `understandContent: ReactNode` prop down through `ConceptPageShell` → `ConceptInteractive`, which renders it for the Understand tab (other tabs keep `TabPlaceholder`). Keeps MDX/`fs` off the client bundle. `TAB_BODY` retyped to `Record<Exclude<ConceptTab,"understand">, …>` so the placeholder map no longer carries a dead understand entry (the `=== "understand"` ternary narrows `activeTab` for the else branch).

New query `getUnderstoodState(userId, conceptId)` (live, per-user, not cached) for the button's initial state.
  - `/api/progress/route.ts` — was a 501 stub (auth + rate-limit only). Implemented the minimal write: parse/validate `{ conceptId, tab }` (tab guarded against `CONCEPT_TABS`), then `onConflictDoUpdate` the matching `{tab}Completed` flag scoped to `session.user.id` via a type-safe `COMPLETED_SET: Record<ConceptTab, Partial<InferInsertModel>>` (no `as`, no dynamic-key insert). Key reframe surfaced during homework: the 4 hero simulators (Features 09-12) already have the *full* control set, so "full version" ≠ "more controls". Built in two slices. Four decisions resolved with the user up front (all the recommended option): (1) **reuse the same components + props** (`SimulatorRootProps`: `showScenarioSwitcher?`, `onReachedEnd?`), not separate "full" components — the homepage Hero keeps its single-scenario, untracked behaviour by simply rendering `<XSimulator />` with no props; (2) **2 scenarios each** (existing + 1 new authored); (3) completion **auto-fires on the final frame, silent for logged-out** (no POST, no redirect), subtle "Simulator completed" indicator on success; (4) the 4 concepts without a simulator show a **"coming soon" empty state**. Scenario-2 content also confirmed up front per the project's simulator-semantics norm: Event Loop → **async/await**, React Rendering → **wasted child re-render** (see follow-up below — originally state batching), Browser Pipeline → **visibility:hidden** (contrast with scenario 1's display:none), CSS Specificity → **!important**.
  - **Integration seam — the learn↔simulators boundary:** `features/learn` may **not** import `features/simulators` (the "features never import other features" invariant). Resolved exactly like the homepage Hero (which lives in `components/` and imports the simulators): new `src/components/simulators/ConceptSimulator.tsx` (a `components/`-layer client wrapper) owns the slug→simulator registry, the completion POST, and the empty state; the server `page.tsx` builds it and passes it down as a `simulateContent: ReactNode` prop through `ConceptPageShell` → `ConceptInteractive` (same children-as-props pattern Feature 22 used for `understandContent`). Simulators stay network-free (an architecture invariant) — they only expose a generic `onReachedEnd` lifecycle callback; all I/O (the `/api/progress` POST, fire-once via a ref, logged-out guard, 429/error handling mirroring `MarkUnderstoodButton`) lives in the host wrapper. `/api/progress` already accepted `tab:"simulate"` since Feature 22, so no API change was needed; added `getSimulateState(userId, conceptId)` (live, per-user) for the initial completed state.

This alone makes every Simulate tab functional with the existing scenario.

The only console error was a single Better-Auth `/api/auth/*` **429** after the test hammered ~7 protected pages in 10s (the documented 5/60s auth limit, not Feature 23 code — that page makes no progress POST and the run was logged-out).   - **Follow-up (React scenario 2 redesign, same session, before any PR):** user reviewed the first React scenario 2 (**state batching**) and rejected it as too similar to scenario 1 — it walked the exact same 5 cards (Counter → Render → VDOM → Diffing → Real DOM) with the only on-screen difference being the count reading `2` instead of `1`; the "batching" idea lived entirely in caption text, so a learner couldn't *see* the difference. Offered three genuinely-distinct replacements (with previews of each end state); user chose **"wasted child re-render"** — a Parent's state change re-runs both Parent and Child render functions, but diffing skips the unchanged Child and only Parent's DOM is patched (teaches render ≠ commit). This is the most visually distinct option because the lesson is about *components* re-running, not HTML element diffing, so it needed a new visual vocabulary rather than reusing the counter trees.
    - Added a `kind: "dom-update" | "wasted-render"` discriminator + `componentLabel`/`stateLabel`/`clickLabel`/`childLabel`/`insight` to `RenderScenario`; scenario 1 keeps the counter `MiniTree` bodies untouched (zero regression risk), scenario 2 swaps the four downstream cards to a new `ComponentTreeView.tsx` (a Parent→Child node pair whose per-card `mode` — rerender/vdom/diff/commit — drives the emphasis: both nodes "re-rendered" in render/vdom, then the Child visibly falls away as "no change"/"untouched" in diff/commit). `StageCardsRow` branches its `bodies` map on `kind`; `ComponentStateBody` took label props (now "Parent Component"/"likes"/"Like"); the React `InsightCallout` + `ExecutionFlowBar`'s first pill ("Click Like") became scenario-driven. The earlier `targetCount`/`count`-into-trees threading from the batching pass stayed (scenario 1 still uses it).
    - Re-verified: `tsc`/`eslint` clean; Playwright (warmed dev route, `networkidle` to ensure hydration before clicking the tab) stepped scenario 2 to the diffing + commit frames — screenshots confirm Render Phase shows Parent **and** Avatar both "re-rendered", Diffing highlights only Parent as "changed" with Avatar faded "no change", Real DOM commit patches only Parent ("Only Parent patched") with Avatar "untouched", the insight reads "Re-rendering is not the same as updating the DOM", and the flow bar's first pill reads "Click Like"; zero console errors. (Note: the initial debug run used `domcontentloaded` and clicked the tab before hydration, so it read the still-mounted Understand panel — a test-timing artifact, fixed by waiting for `networkidle`, not an app bug.)
    - **Follow-up 2 (visual rework of the same scenario, same session):** user said the first `ComponentTreeView` pass (abstract labelled boxes + small text status badges) "is not good" and asked to make it genuinely visual. Reworked the two nodes into concrete UI: **Post** is a little card with a `Heart` icon + a "likes" count (the one thing that ever changes on screen), **Avatar** is a profile circle (`User` glyph) rendered **pixel-identical in every card** — only its emphasis ring/dim changes, never its content, which is the lesson made visible. Per-card visuals: Render Phase shows both nodes pulsing with a "⚡ render() ran" tag (both functions executed); Virtual DOM shows both as a fresh snapshot; **Diffing is now an explicit before→after diff** — `❤0 → ❤1 changed` (streak) on the Post row vs `👤 = 👤 ✓ same` (muted) on the Avatar row; Real DOM (commit) pops the Post's count to 1 ("DOM patched") while the Avatar sits faded ("DOM untouched"). Verified via the same Playwright flow — screenshots of the render/diff/commit frames confirm the concrete UI reads clearly at the card's narrow width; `tsc`/`eslint` clean, zero console errors.

Homework surfaced two facts that shaped the whole feature: (a) the seeded `challenges.test_cases` are **descriptive labels, not executable code**, so a real "Run Tests" needs executable assertions that didn't exist; (b) **no challenge was linked to a concept** (`ChallengeSeed` had no `conceptId`), so the Challenge tab had nothing to render per concept. Three decisions resolved with the user up front: (1) **real iframe sandbox + real executable tests** (the engine is reused by Build/Practice later), not UI-first cosmetic; (2) **link all 3 seeded challenges to concepts + empty states elsewhere** (`implement-debounce`→event-loop, `virtual-list`→react-rendering, `specificity-calculator`→css-specificity), premium-gate seam stubbed for F38; (3) **JS-only sandbox now, re-scope `virtual-list`** to test the pure windowing function (`visibleRange(...)` — the real lesson) instead of mounting React, deferring the heavier React/JSX-in-iframe engine to F26. Test-spec storage (not asked, established-pattern default): DB `test_cases` stay display labels; executable assertions live in an in-code registry keyed by slug (off the client until needed), same precedent as F22's server-MDX / F23's slug→simulator registry.
  - **Sandbox engine** — new self-contained `src/features/practice/sandbox/`: `buildSandboxDoc.ts` (builds the iframe `srcdoc` — a self-clearing timeout guard + `error` listener, the user code in its own `<script>` so a syntax error there can't stop the harness and its declarations become globals, then an authored-tests runner that posts one `RESULT`; injects `assert`/`assertEqual`/deep-`eq`/`delay` helpers; escapes `</script>` in user code; every message carries a per-run `nonce`), `runInSandbox.ts` (creates a hidden `sandbox="allow-scripts"` iframe — never `allow-same-origin` — listens on `message` filtered by nonce, 6s parent timeout > the inner 5s guard, always tears down before resolving, resolves once and never rejects), `useSandbox.ts` (React wrapper: `idle|running|done` status, last result, `run()` returns the result + ignores re-entrant runs), `testSpecs.ts` (`getTestSpec(slug)` → `SandboxTest[]`, labels matched 1:1 to the seeded `test_cases[].label`), `types.ts`, `index.ts`. Follows `security.md` → Code Execution Security exactly (allow-scripts only, postMessage only, timeout). Known limitation (inherent to the iframe approach `security.md` prescribes, not a Worker): a synchronous infinite loop in user code can't be interrupted by the inner `setTimeout` guard — acceptable for short challenge code; the parent timeout still resolves the UI.

(2) `@monaco-editor/react` does **not** fire `onChange` for a programmatic `editor.setValue()` (the controlled `value` stays stale, so the editor showed the solution while `code` state stayed at the starter → a misleading 0/3) — real user typing does fire it, confirmed by switching the test to `Input.insertText`. Both were test-harness artifacts, not app defects.

Root cause: the banner was driven by `succeeded = completed || passed`, so a *previously*-completed challenge (persisted `challengeCompleted` in the DB, or a pass earlier in the session) kept the green banner regardless of the current code. Fixed: the green banner is now driven by the **current run only** (`passed`); a previously-completed challenge instead shows a distinct neutral "you've completed this — run again any time" pill that disappears the moment a new run starts, so it can never contradict a failing panel. In-browser (CDP): the rich description renders (headings/code-example/blockquote present), and typing in the demo drives the "your debounce" lane live (5 keys → 5 CALLs end-to-end; lane counter updates). **Dev-cache gotcha confirmed and worked around:** `getChallengeByConceptId` is `unstable_cache`d (disk-backed under `.next/cache`), so the rewritten description stayed stale across a plain process restart — required clearing `.next` to surface it in dev (production rebuilds fetch fresh). Resolved as a deliberate forward-link (same precedent as `MoreInterviewPrepCta` → `/interview-prep` ahead of Phase 6), not by building the real pricing/Stripe page now (that's Phase 9's scope, not requested here).
    - **`BuildPremiumLocked` gained an `isLoggedIn` prop** and a `bg-premium`/`text-premium-foreground` CTA button (`ui-tokens.md`'s already-documented "Premium CTA" style — first real usage of that spec). Logged-in → links straight to `/pricing`. Logged-out → links to `/login?callbackURL=/pricing`. `ConceptBuild.tsx` threads its existing `isLoggedIn` prop through on the early-return branch.
    - **Real gap found while wiring the logged-out path, not just cosmetic:** `OAuthButton`'s `callbackURL` was hardcoded to `"/learn"`, so a logged-out user clicking through from a locked Build project would always land back on `/learn` after signing in, losing the "they were trying to reach pricing" intent entirely. Fixed properly, not with a one-off special case: `OAuthButton` now accepts an optional `callbackURL` prop (default `"/learn"`, so every existing call site is unchanged); the login page reads a `?callbackURL=` search param and threads the validated value to both `OAuthButton` and the already-logged-in bounce (`redirect(...)`, previously hardcoded to `"/"`).
    - **Security consideration surfaced by this change, handled before shipping, not after:** `?callbackURL=` is an untrusted query param controlling a redirect destination — the textbook open-redirect shape. Added `getSafeRedirectPath(path, fallback)` (`features/auth/lib/getSafeRedirectPath.ts`, same file-placement precedent as `getLoginErrorMessage`): rejects anything not starting with a single `/` (absolute URLs, protocol-relative `//host`, and the `/\host` backslash variant some browsers normalize the same way) and falls back to the caller's own existing default instead of ever passing an unsafe value through.
    - **Verified live, not just by reading the code:** real browser (Playwright, network-intercepted `signIn.social()` calls rather than trusting the UI alone): clicking the locked state's CTA while logged out lands on `/login?callbackURL=%2Fpricing`; the real outgoing Better-Auth request carries `callbackURL: "/pricing"`; visiting `/login?callbackURL=https://evil.example.com` directly and clicking "Continue with Google" sends `callbackURL: "/learn"` to Better-Auth — the malicious value is discarded, not forwarded. Typecheck/lint clean project-wide. **Two more leaked-Chrome-process rounds hit during this verification** (same root cause as the main entry's Follow-up 2 note — crashed scripts missing `try/finally`) — killed them each time; a `page.waitForURL()` race (reading `page.url()` immediately after a client-side navigation click, before the route actually changed) was the one real test-script bug, fixed by awaiting the URL change instead of a fixed timeout.
    - **Docs updated:** `ui-registry.md` (`BuildPremiumLocked`'s CTA, the `callbackURL`/`getSafeRedirectPath` mechanism under both `OAuthButton` and the Build Components section), this entry.
  - **Follow-up 4 (user-reported bug, same session): logged-in premium account intermittently showed the logged-out Navbar for 1-2 minutes after navigating through the new login→pricing flow.** Investigated rather than assumed — diagnosed as a real, pre-existing auth-infrastructure bug this feature's new flow just made easy to trigger, not something the CTA/redirect code itself did wrong.
    - **Root cause, confirmed empirically, not guessed:** Better-Auth's own internal rate limiter (`lib/auth/server.ts`, set up in Feature 19: global `{ window: 60, max: 5 }`) applies to *every* Better-Auth endpoint including `/get-session` — a read-only check of the caller's own already-issued cookie, not a brute-forceable action like sign-in. `curl` in a tight loop confirmed the 6th request within 60s reliably returned `429`. Read Better-Auth's actual client-side session-store source (`node_modules/better-auth/dist/client/session-atom.mjs`) rather than assuming: on a non-401 error it correctly *preserves* the last-known session data — but a fresh full-page navigation (Build tab → CTA → `/login` → homepage is exactly this: several real page loads in a row, each remounting the session store from scratch) has no prior data to preserve. If that page's very first `get-session` call happens to land on the tripped limiter, the Navbar shows logged-out until the 60s window resets (sometimes closer to 2 minutes if the user's own refreshes re-trip it). **This exact 429 was already visible once before and dismissed as harmless** — Feature 25's progress-tracker note mentions "six rapid headless page loads" tripping it during automated verification — but a real user hitting it through ordinary browsing turns it from test noise into a visible product bug.
    - **Fix:** added `rateLimit.customRules: { "/get-session": { window: 60, max: 100 } }` in `lib/auth/server.ts` — confirmed via Better-Auth's own source (`node_modules/@better-auth/core/src/types/init-options.ts` for the option shape, `node_modules/better-auth/dist/api/rate-limiter/index.mjs` for how `customRules` resolves per-path, and that Better-Auth's own built-in "special rules" already give sign-in/sign-up a separate tighter window — `/get-session` isn't one of them, so it was silently falling back to the app's blanket default). Left the global `max: 5` untouched — it still protects sign-in/sign-up.
    - **Verified live:** before the fix, a `curl` loop 429'd on request 6; after, 12 rapid requests all returned `200`, and a longer 90-request burst confirmed the new ceiling still caps out (around the 89th/90th request, consistent with `max: 100`) — so this is a real, working override, not just a config value that looks right on paper.
    - **Docs updated:** `architecture.md` (new "Better-Auth's Own Rate Limiter" subsection under Rate Limiting Pattern — this mechanism was previously undocumented entirely, a real gap, not just this fix), this entry.

Three decisions resolved with the user up front, all the recommended option: (1) tab-completion XP is only awarded on a false→true transition of that tab's flag (fetch-then-compare, not blind upsert) — repeat POSTs stay a no-op; (2) the streak bonus fires once per UTC calendar day (first activity that day), not on every POST; (3) all writes (progress upsert, xp_events inserts, profiles update) run inside a single `db.transaction()` — the first transaction in the codebase, so a mid-request failure can't leave XP awarded without the progress flag set or vice versa. Two more gaps surfaced during homework, also resolved with the user: `architecture.md`'s "+50 first-time concept completion bonus" had no matching `xp_events.event_type` — added `concept_completed` to `XP_EVENT_TYPES` (required a migration widening the `xp_events` CHECK constraint); and `streak_last_activity` day comparisons use the server's UTC calendar day (no per-user timezone system exists).

Takes the transaction handle + `userId`/`conceptId`/`tab`: `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE`s the existing `user_concept_progress` row (locks it for the transaction's duration so two concurrent completions of the same tab can't both observe "not yet completed" and double-award), upserts the tab's flag, awards that tab's XP only if the flag was previously false, sets `fullyCompleted`/`completedAt` + awards the +50 bonus the first time all 5 flags are true, then `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE`s the `profiles` row and runs the streak math (same-day → no change; yesterday → `streakCurrent + 1`; older/null → reset to 1; `streakLongest = max(...)`) awarding +5 XP only when the day actually changed, and finally increments `profiles.xp` by the request's total.

Conclusion: the code and the harness mechanism are both correct, but the original 5s inner / 6s parent timeout budget left too little headroom over the ~2s real baseline once genuine dev-mode main-thread contention (Turbopack HMR, React, Monaco all sharing one thread with the sandboxed iframe) is added — a margin problem, not a logic bug. **Fix:** widened `buildSandboxDoc`'s inner guard 5000ms → 12000ms and `runInSandbox`'s parent cap 6000ms → 13000ms (`src/lib/sandbox/`) — affects both Practice's Challenge tab and Build tab, the two consumers of this shared engine. "knew" : "review"`, returns a plain `Record<string, "knew"|"review">` rather than importing `features/interview-prep`'s `QuestionRating` type (the same "features never import features" boundary Feature 25 already had to fix once).

    - **Critical, fixed — silently swallowed error in `ConceptInterview.tsx`'s rating POST:** `void fetch(...).catch(() => {})` did nothing on failure — no log, no user feedback, no retry — directly violating `error-handling.md`'s "errors are never silently swallowed" invariant, and meaning a concept could show "fully completed" while a specific rating silently never saved. **Fix:** extracted the fetch into `postRating()`, which `console.error`s on a non-OK response or network error and tracks failed question ids in a new `failedRatingIds` state; `QuestionCard` gained an optional `saveFailed` prop rendering a small inline `text-error` note ("Couldn't save this rating — pick a rating again to retry") next to the rating buttons — re-picking a rating naturally retries since the route is a safe upsert, not fire-once.
    - **Important, fixed — `code-standards.md`'s `XP_EVENT_TYPES` example was stale**, still showing the pre-Feature-27 8 values under "never use any other string." Added `concept_completed` with a comment noting when/why.

    - **Important, fixed — both `/api/progress` and `/api/interview-rating`'s catch-all 500 handlers never logged the underlying error** (`catch { return Response.json(...) }`), contradicting `error-handling.md`'s documented pattern and Logging Strategy — a DB write failure in production would have been completely unobserved server-side. **Fixed properly, not naively:** rather than logging the raw `error` object (which is what `error-handling.md`'s own example does), added a shared `getPostgresErrorCode(error)` helper (`src/lib/dbErrors.ts`) that extracts only the Postgres error code from `error.cause` — mirroring the *safer* precedent already established in `lib/auth/provisionProfile.ts` (Feature 16), where logging a raw `DrizzleQueryError` was found to leak the full insert payload (name/email/avatar URL) into logs. Both routes now log `[route] DB write failed: <code>` before returning the generic 500.

  - **Follow-up 7 (CodeRabbit review on PR #44, verified each finding against current code before touching anything):** 2 inline + 1 real correctness bug fixed, 1 nitpick fixed, 2 findings skipped with a verified reason.
    - **Fixed — `/api/progress` and `/api/interview-rating` shared one rate-limit bucket.** Both called `ratelimit.limit(session.user.id)` with the bare user id as the key — heavy use of one endpoint could exhaust the other's quota. Namespaced each: `` `progress:${session.user.id}` `` / `` `interview-rating:${session.user.id}` ``.
    - **Fixed — AppNavbar's XP/streak went stale after a completion, only updating on the next full navigation** (by original design — no client polling — but a real point of confusion, matching what the user hit this session going from 155→185 XP with no visible change until refresh). Added `router.refresh()` after every successful `/api/progress` completion POST, across all 5 tab hosts (`MarkUnderstoodButton`, `ConceptSimulator`, `ConceptChallenge`, `ConceptBuild`, `ConceptInterview`) — re-fetches `(app)/layout.tsx`'s server data (including the Navbar's `initialXp`/`initialStreak`) without losing client state.
    - **Fixed — real race condition in `applyProgressUpdate`, confirmed by reproducing it, not just by reading the code.** `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` only locks rows that already exist — a user's very first-ever action on a concept has no row yet, so two truly concurrent first completions could both read "no row" before either lock applied, and both award XP. **Fix:** an `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` now runs before the `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE`, guaranteeing the row exists by the time it's locked — the insert itself is the synchronization point, since Postgres serializes concurrent inserts targeting the same unique `(userId, conceptId)` key. **Verified against the real DB with genuine concurrency** (two separate pool connections, two full `db.transaction()` calls fired via `Promise.all` at the same instant, same brand-new concept/tab pair neither had ever touched): before trusting the fix, confirmed the scenario was reachable; after the fix, exactly 1 `xp_events` row was written, not 2. Re-ran the standard 7-scenario regression suite too — all still pass.
    - **Fixed (nitpick) — the 5 tab-state getters (`getUnderstoodState`/`getSimulateState`/`getChallengeState`/`getInterviewState`/`getBuildState`) each queried `user_concept_progress` separately**, even though `page.tsx` always calls all 5 with the identical `(userId, conceptId)` in one `Promise.all` — 5 round trips for 1 row. Consolidated behind a `cache()`-wrapped `getTabProgressRow(userId, conceptId)` (`queries/tabState.ts`) that fetches all 5 completion columns once; each getter now just plucks its own boolean. Every existing call site/signature unchanged. Verified the consolidated query returns the correct 5 flags against real data (Event Loop, all true).
    - **Skipped, verified not a bug — "streak date mixing" (Date vs YYYY-MM-DD string).** Checked Drizzle's actual behavior rather than assuming: `profiles.streakLastActivity` is declared as a bare `date("streak_last_activity")` with no `{ mode: "date" }` override, and Drizzle's own `date()` overloads default to **string** mode when no mode is given (confirmed both by reading `drizzle-orm/pg-core/columns/date.d.ts` and by a `tsc` type probe: assigning a string literal to `profiles.streakLastActivity`'s inferred type compiles clean). So the column is genuinely `string | null` at both the type and runtime level, matching `todayUtc()`/`yesterdayUtc()`'s string returns exactly — the comparison in `applyProgressUpdate` was already correct; there was no Date/string mismatch to fix.
    - **Skipped, not worth it at this stage — migration `0008`'s `ADD CONSTRAINT ... CHECK` should use `NOT VALID` + a separate `VALIDATE CONSTRAINT` to avoid a full-table scan/lock.** Correct general Postgres advice, but: (1) this migration is already applied to the dev DB and committed — rewriting it after the fact violates migration immutability (the right move, if ever needed, is a *new* migration, not editing 0008); (2) `xp_events` currently has a handful of dev-seed rows, so the lock/scan this guards against doesn't exist yet at this pre-launch stage. Left as-is; worth revisiting as a real migration if/when this table has production scale.
    - **Re-verified after all fixes:** `tsc --noEmit` + `eslint` clean project-wide. Real dev server: homepage and both API routes' 401 paths still correct with the new namespaced rate-limit keys.

Needed because this feature renumbers the 8 pre-Phase-10 concepts' `orderIndex` to their new position in the final teaching order (e.g. `event-loop` moves from position 1 → 9 within `javascript-runtime`), which only takes effect on re-seed with an update-capable upsert.

Slugs unchanged (no URL/FK impact).

Was cosmetic-only on 8 concepts; now visibly wrong on 76. Worth a dedicated pass, not part of Feature 40's data-only scope.
  - **Follow-up (`/review` skill, 1 Important finding, fixed):** the `concepts` `onConflictDoUpdate` set its `isPremium` column from the proposed insert row's value, but `ConceptSeed` never exposed an `isPremium` field, so every reseed silently forced `is_premium` back to its column default (`false`) for all 76 rows — confirmed live-facing, not dead, since `CategoryAccordionItem.tsx` already renders a `Lock` icon off `concept.isPremium`. Harmless today (no concept has ever been premium, and Phase 10 doesn't need one to be), but would have silently clobbered any future out-of-band premium flag the moment someone reran the seed. **Fixed:** added `isPremium?: boolean` to `ConceptSeed`, mirroring `ChallengeSeed`/`ProjectBriefSeed`'s existing optional-if-free convention. Re-ran the seed to confirm it's stable (no concept's `isPremium` changed). `tsc`/`eslint` clean.
  - **Follow-up (user-reported after merge, on a dev server that had been running continuously across this feature's several reseeds):** a brand-new concept page 404'd, plus an unrelated-looking auth glitch (appeared logged out, logging back in bounced to the homepage still showing "Log In"). Diagnosed rather than assumed: `curl` to the 404'ing route reproduced it live while a direct DB query confirmed the row existed correctly (right slug, right category) — a stale-cache-read problem, not a data problem. Root cause: `getConceptBySlug`/`getConceptCatalog` are `unstable_cache`-wrapped (`tags: ["concepts"], revalidate: 3600`), and `scripts/seed.ts` is a standalone process with no way to call `revalidateTag("concepts")` on an already-running dev server — a slug queried before the seed added/renamed it stays cached as a miss for up to an hour. Restarting the dev server (clears Next's in-memory Data Cache) resolved both the 404 and the auth glitch in one step. Documented under Notes below so a future reseed-while-running doesn't get mis-diagnosed as a real regression.

  - **Decisions confirmed with the user up front (via `architect`):** (1) Challenge is selective, not mandatory per concept — skipped for `esm-vs-commonjs` and `memory-management-leaks`, neither a natural executable-code exercise in the browser sandbox; (2) Build (project brief) is mandatory — all 13 new concepts get one, no skipping; (3) premium tier rule for Build: beginner difficulty → free, intermediate/advanced → premium; (4) premium tier for Challenge follows `project-overview.md`'s actual documented gate ("Advanced challenges") rather than the Build rule — only the 2 advanced-difficulty challenges (`implement-curry`, `implement-take`) are premium; (5) Interview questions stay free across the board — `project-overview.md` only fully gates the FF System Design collection, not individual questions elsewhere; (6) `implement-debounce` re-linked from `event-loop` to `debouncing-throttling` (`conceptSlug` change via the existing `onConflictDoUpdate` re-seed), matching `build-plan.md`'s explicit note that the two pair together — `event-loop` got a brand-new replacement challenge (`predict-execution-order`) instead of losing its Challenge tab.
  - **A broader premium-gating rule the user proposed** — "beginner concepts fully free across all 5 tabs, intermediate/advanced concepts fully locked across all 5 tabs, platform-wide" — was surfaced as out of scope for this feature (it needs new gating code for Understand/Simulate/Challenge/Interview, none of which have concept-level premium gates today; only Build does) and deferred to Feature 38 (Premium Content Gating) instead of built here.
  - **Content shipped:** 13 new Understand MDX guides (`content/concepts/javascript-runtime/*.mdx`, exact `event-loop.mdx` frontmatter contract and heading conventions); 11 new `CHALLENGES` entries — 10 for new concepts (`esm-vs-commonjs` and `memory-management-leaks` skipped at this point) plus a replacement for `event-loop` (`predict-execution-order`), since `implement-debounce` moved to `debouncing-throttling`; 6 previously-orphaned `INTERVIEW_QUESTIONS` entries (no `conceptSlug`) discovered and re-linked to the correct new concept instead of duplicated (`null`/`undefined`, `==`/`===` → `equality-type-coercion`; prototypal inheritance, closures, `Promise` combinators, `this` → their matching new concepts) plus 72 new questions (12 `ff-75` flagships + 60 `ff-javascript` top-ups); 13 new Project briefs (`PROJECT_BRIEFS`).
  - **Verified (at this point in the feature, before the `find-leaked-listeners` follow-up below):** `npx tsx scripts/seed.ts` — 76 concepts (unchanged), 14 challenges (3 → 14, i.e. the 11 new entries above), 104 interview questions (32 → 104), 17 project briefs (4 → 17); re-ran once more to confirm idempotency (identical counts, no duplicates). `tsc --noEmit` + `eslint` clean. Live dev server (killed a stale pre-reseed instance and cleared `.next` first, per Feature 40's documented stale-cache gotcha): spot-checked concept pages across all 3 difficulty tiers — sidebar shows the correct 14-item JS Runtime ordering, MDX guide/Challenge/Interview/Build content all render correctly for both a free (`hoisting-temporal-dead-zone`) and a premium-tier (`generators-iterators`) concept, no console/server errors.
  - **Follow-up (`/review` skill, 1 Important finding, fixed):** the `auto-bound-event-emitter` project brief (`this-binding-execution-context`) was titled and framed around guaranteeing correct `this` binding, but its solution never actually touched `this` — `emit` called every handler as a bare `handler(payload)`, and none of the 3 test cases exercised `this` either. **Fixed:** `on(event, handler, context)` now takes an optional `context`, and `emit` always invokes via `handler.call(context, payload)` — a bare method reference (`dash.handleLogin`) genuinely keeps the right `this` with no manual `.bind()` by the caller, which is what the brief always claimed. Test cases rewritten to actually verify it (independent `this` per handler, plus the no-context fallback). Re-verified: `tsc`/`eslint` clean, seed idempotent, fix confirmed live in the DB.
  - **Follow-up (user-reported after reviewing the live page):** `memory-management-leaks` originally had no Challenge — a deliberate planning decision ("memory leak detection isn't unit-testable in a browser sandbox"). User asked for it to have one anyway, same as every other concept. **Added `find-leaked-listeners`** — reframed the exercise around the one leak pattern that *is* cleanly testable without a real heap: given a chronological log of `addEventListener`/`removeEventListener` calls, find the ids added more times than removed (a bookkeeping problem, not a GC-observation problem). `medium` difficulty, premium (matches the advanced-concept-challenges-are-premium rule). This brings `CHALLENGES` to **15 total (3 pre-existing + 12 new: the 11 above plus this one)**, re-confirmed via `npx tsx scripts/seed.ts` ("15 challenge(s) upserted"). `esm-vs-commonjs` remains the one concept in this category with no Challenge — revisit if the same request comes up for it.
  - **Follow-up (user-reported: "Run Tests" not working for any concept's Challenge or Build tab) — a real architectural gap this feature's planning missed entirely.** `getTestSpec`/`getBuildTestSpec` (`src/features/practice/sandbox/testSpecs.ts`, `src/features/build/data/testSpecs.ts`) are hardcoded, code-side `Record<slug, SandboxTest[]>` maps — the DB's `test_cases` column is display-labels only (documented in both files' own header comments, missed during this feature's research/planning). None of the 12 new Challenges or 13 new Project briefs had an entry, so every one fell back to "Automated tests for this challenge are coming soon," regardless of concept. **This means Challenge/Build content is not actually fully data-driven the way Understand/Interview are** — a correction to carry into Features 42–48: each new Challenge/Project brief needs a matching hand-written `SandboxTest[]` entry (real executable `assert`/`assertEqual`/`delay` source, keyed by slug), not just a seed.ts row. **Fixed:** wrote real test specs for all 25 new items. Verified for real, not just by inspection — a standalone Node harness (reproducing the sandbox's exact `eq`/`assert`/`assertEqual`/`delay` helpers) ran all 79 assertions against the actual `solutionCode` for every one (all passed), and a deliberately wrong solution was confirmed to fail (ruling out a vacuous/always-passing harness). `tsc`/`eslint` clean on both files. Confirmed live: the previously-missing test labels now appear in `hoisting-temporal-dead-zone`'s page data and the "coming soon" fallback is gone.
  - **Follow-up (CodeRabbit review on PR #48, 2 findings, both fixed):** `array-object-methods-immutability.mdx`'s `whatsHappening` grouped `Object.entries`/`Object.keys` with spread as if all three produce a non-mutating object copy — `Object.entries`/`Object.keys` actually return arrays, not object copies, only spread does. Reworded so only spread is described that way. Separately, `progress-tracker.md`'s own Challenge-count arithmetic didn't reconcile: "11 new + event-loop's replacement" double-counted the replacement (already one of the 11), and the `find-leaked-listeners` follow-up above never updated the running total. Fixed both entries to state the correct 11 new at the initial pass (3 → 14) and the final 15 total (3 → 12 new) after `find-leaked-listeners`, matching the actual `CHALLENGES` array and the seed script's own logged output.

  - **Content shipped:** 10 new Understand MDX guides (`content/concepts/css/*.mdx`, including `css-specificity.mdx`); 9 new `CHALLENGES` entries (35 → 44) with matching `SandboxTest[]` entries in `src/features/practice/sandbox/testSpecs.ts`; 45 new `INTERVIEW_QUESTIONS` (199 → 244, 5 per new concept, all `ff-75` since CSS has no dedicated collection the way JS/React do — same pattern Feature 42 used for Browser Internals); 9 new `PROJECT_BRIEFS` (36 → 45) with matching `SandboxTest[]` entries in `src/features/build/data/testSpecs.ts` — each Build brief extends its Challenge's core function into a slightly larger "wire this into a real tool" utility (e.g. the stacking-order Challenge's single "topmost element" check becomes the Build brief's full bottom-to-top paint-order resolver), matching Feature 26/41's established pattern.

One extraction bug caught and fixed mid-verification: the harness's first pass did a naive raw-text slice between backticks, which broke on the two solutions containing a nested template literal (`` `Unknown unit: ${unit}` ``, escaped as `` \`...\${unit}\` `` inside the outer `solutionCode` template literal) — fixed by re-evaluating the extracted slice through the real JS template-literal grammar (`` eval("`" + raw + "`") ``) instead of hand-rolling the unescaping. `npx tsx scripts/seed.ts` — 76 concepts (unchanged), 44 challenges (35 → 44), 244 interview questions (199 → 244), 45 project briefs (36 → 45); re-ran to confirm idempotency (identical counts). `tsc --noEmit` + `eslint` clean.   - **Follow-up (`/review` skill, 1 finding, fixed):** `css-specificity.mdx`'s "Ties and `!important`" section overstated that `!important` bypasses specificity entirely ("a fight specificity was never actually deciding"), contradicting `the-cascade-inheritance.mdx`'s own correct wording that specificity is always the tiebreaker *within* a given importance tier. **Fixed:** reworded to state that `!important` only elevates a declaration into a higher-priority tier — two competing `!important` declarations still resolve by specificity, then source order, exactly like two normal declarations would.

  - **Follow-up (inline review comments, 2 findings, both fixed):** `designing-real-time-updates.mdx`'s `keyInsight` claimed SSE "gets full-duplex's benefits," which is backwards — SSE is one-way server-to-client push only; full-duplex describes WebSockets, not SSE. **Fixed:** reworded to describe SSE accurately (one-way push over a single plain-HTTP connection) while keeping the direction-vs-frequency guidance and the WebSocket-full-duplex contrast intact. Separately, `dedupe-and-cache-fetcher`'s `createFetcher` only deleted a key from `inflight` inside the success (`.then`) callback — a rejected `fetchFn(key)` left that key stuck in `inflight` permanently, so every subsequent `get(key)` call would return the same stale rejected promise forever instead of retrying. **Fixed:** moved the `inflight.delete(key)` cleanup into `.finally()` so it runs on both the resolve and reject paths. Verified directly against the real extracted `solutionCode` (not just inspection): a fetcher whose `fetchFn` rejects on the first call and succeeds on the second now correctly rejects the first `get()`, then successfully retries and resolves the second — confirming the fix, not just the absence of a crash. Still not pushed — another round of user sign-off pending before the logic pass.
  - **Follow-up 2 (env bug, then two more UI rounds, same session, before any push/PR):**
    1. **Found a real environment bug, not a code regression:** the user reported the previous round's fixes (list style, Company filter) weren't visible — screenshots showed the old grid design with no Company filter. Investigated rather than assuming the code was wrong: `netstat -ano` showed **two zombie dev server processes** squatting on ports 3000 and 3001, left over from earlier in this session — `pkill -f "next dev"` had silently failed to match them (Windows/Git Bash spawns `npm run dev` as a bare `node` process; the pattern never matched its argv), so the user's browser had been hitting a pre-fix server the entire time. Killed both by their actual port-bound PID (`netstat` → PID → `taskkill //F //PID`, not by name pattern) and started one clean instance; confirmed via fresh screenshot that the current code already had everything from the prior round.
    2. **Numbering + row redesign:** once looking at the real current state, user still didn't like the row design and asked for numbering. Added a numbered circle badge (same shape as `QuestionCard`'s index circle from Interview Prep) that swaps to a green `Check` once solved, and moved the difficulty pill onto the title's own line (previously a 3rd column spanning the full row height) so the row reads as one tight primary line + one metadata line instead of a 3-column spread.
    3. **Real mobile overflow bug, caught by testing the redesign at 390px before shipping it (not by the user reporting it):** the difficulty badge rendered partially cut off at the viewport edge ("Ea", "Ha", "Medi"). Root-caused with `getBoundingClientRect()` walked up the full DOM chain from the row to `<html>` rather than guessed at — a classic flexbox gap: a flex item's default `min-width` is its content's natural width, not `0`, so the title never actually shrank low enough for `truncate` to engage, even with `min-w-0 flex-1` present on intermediate wrappers. The overflow surfaced 3 levels up at the page's own `mx-auto max-w-4xl px-6` container (rendering wider than the 390px viewport) and was invisible to a plain `document.documentElement.scrollWidth` check because `<body>` has `overflow-x: clip`, silently absorbing it. **Fixed properly, not by re-guessing more `min-w-0` placements:** switched the row's two split points to CSS Grid with explicit `grid-cols-[auto_minmax(0,1fr)]` / `grid-cols-[minmax(0,1fr)_auto]` — a `minmax(0, 1fr)` track has no min-content floor the way a flex item does, so this is a structural fix, not a workaround. Re-verified via the same DOM-chain measurement (every ancestor now reports exactly 390px, no silent overflow) and a fresh screenshot (full difficulty text visible, long titles truncate with an ellipsis instead of pushing content off-screen).
    - Re-verified after this follow-up: `tsc --noEmit` + `eslint` clean; desktop + mobile screenshots confirm the numbered rows and the overflow fix; a full console-error + overflow check across `/practice`, `/practice/javascript-runtime`, `/practice/react` came back clean (0 errors, `scrollWidth === clientWidth`). Dev server stopped cleanly via its real PID this time (not by name pattern) to avoid leaving another zombie. Still not pushed — pending user sign-off.
  - **Follow-up 3 (alignment + navigation, same session, before any push/PR):** 3 more findings against the running server.
    1. **Vertical alignment fixed:** the number badge and difficulty pill were pinned to the top of the row (`items-start` at the outer split, with the difficulty pill nested inside the title's own line) rather than centered against the row's actual height, which visibly reads as "off-center" once a row's second line (concept + company chips) adds height. Restructured to a proper 3-column grid — `grid-cols-[auto_minmax(0,1fr)_auto] items-center` — with the number badge and difficulty pill as separate outer columns from the (variable-height) content column, so both now center against its full height regardless of whether the content wraps to one or two lines. Re-verified the grid restructure didn't reopen the mobile overflow bug fixed in Follow-up 2: re-ran the same `getBoundingClientRect()` ancestor-chain check (every level still reports exactly the viewport width) and re-screenshotted mobile.
    2. **"Why are some questions ticked without attempting them?" — clarified as intended mock behavior, not a bug.** `completed: true` on 2 of `mockPracticeData.ts`'s challenges is placeholder data standing in for real per-user progress, deliberately mixed (solved + unsolved) so both visual states could be verified during this UI-first pass — there is no real attempt-tracking yet (that's `user_challenge_submissions`, part of the deferred logic pass). Explained back to the user rather than silently changed; no code change made pending their preference (leave the mixed demo data, or flip everything to unsolved until the logic pass lands).
    3. **Added a back button.** `PracticeBreadcrumb` now renders a circular `ArrowLeft` button to `/practice` immediately left of the `Practice > {Category}` trail. **Iterated twice more on it, same session:** first pass was too small and sat flush against "Practice" — sized up to `h-9 w-9`/`h-5 w-5` icon and given `gap-5` from the trail; user then said they didn't want the circular border that first resize had added — removed, leaving a plain arrow with only a hover-state background circle.
    - Re-verified: `tsc --noEmit` + `eslint` clean; desktop + mobile screenshots confirm centered alignment and the back button; DOM ancestor-chain width check confirms no overflow reintroduced; console-error check clean. Dev server stopped via PID each round.
  - **User confirmed the UI-first pass is good.** Still not pushed — next steps are the logic pass (real `challenges`/`concepts` join for category filtering, `user_challenge_submissions` aggregation for solved state, resolving the still-open mock-completion-data question from Follow-up 3.2) and then push + open the PR.
  - **Follow-up 4 (Category List page redesign against a supplied reference screenshot, same session, before any push/PR):** user opened `build-plan.md` in the IDE (unrelated) and, separately, shared a screenshot of a differently-styled interview-question tracker (dark theme, full-width search, plain-numbered rows with a trailing checkbox) asking to match its search bar, row/card layout, and numbering style — explicitly scoped to layout, not a request to copy its dark theme or its content domain (quizzes/interview trivia vs. this feature's coding challenges).
    1. **Search bar:** moved from `w-full sm:max-w-xs` sitting inline with the filters to a full-width `h-11` input on its own row, matching the reference.
    2. **Filters below search, all on one row:** the difficulty/status pill-groups + Company dropdown moved out of the same flex row as the search box into their own `flex flex-wrap` row underneath it — this is also what fixes "Company wraps to its own line": with the search box no longer eating horizontal space in that row, all 3 filter groups comfortably fit on one line at normal widths.
    3. **Row redesign:** `ChallengeListRow` changed from one shared `divide-y` list box to a `flex flex-col gap-2` stack of individually-bordered row cards (`rounded-lg border`, matching the reference's separated-card look); numbering switched from a circular badge to plain muted zero-padded text (`01`, `02`, `tabular-nums`); completion moved off the number badge entirely onto its own `h-5 w-5` checkbox square at the far right (filled `success` + check when solved, empty bordered square otherwise) — so the row is now `[number] [title/tags] [difficulty pill] [checkbox]`, a 4-column grid.
    - **Applied the overflow lesson from 2 follow-ups ago without re-deriving it:** built the new 4-column row directly with `grid-cols-[auto_minmax(0,1fr)_auto_auto] items-center` from the start, then still verified rather than assumed — re-ran the same `getBoundingClientRect()` ancestor-chain check (every level from the row up to `<html>` reports the exact viewport width, no silent overflow) and fresh mobile/desktop screenshots before calling it done.
    - Re-verified: `tsc --noEmit` + `eslint` clean; desktop + mobile screenshots match the reference's layout; console-error + overflow check across the category page came back clean. Dev server stopped via PID. Still not pushed.
  - **Follow-up 5 (real width bug, found while chasing what looked like a simple "make it wider" request, same session, before any push/PR):** user compared the same reference screenshot again and correctly flagged the search bar/rows as noticeably narrower than the reference, calling the page "very compact."
    1. **First pass (wrong fix, caught before declaring done):** read `ui-rules.md` → Layout and found both pages were using Learn Index's narrow `max-w-4xl` (~900px reading-column exception) instead of the platform's actual general page standard, `max-w-7xl` (1280px, documented in `ui-rules.md`). Bumped both pages' container to `max-w-7xl px-6 lg:px-8`. **Screenshotted before reporting it fixed — it looked identical to before, not wider at all.**
    2. **Root-caused rather than re-guessing a bigger number:** measured every ancestor's width via `getBoundingClientRect()` (same technique as the earlier row-overflow bug) and found the page's own content div rendered at ~613px — completely ignoring both the old and new `max-width` values. Cause: the div is a direct child of `AppLayout`'s `<main className="flex flex-1 flex-col">`, making it a flex item in a column flex container. A flex item with `mx-auto` (auto cross-axis margins) but no explicit `width` doesn't stretch under the default `align-items: stretch` — auto margins disable stretch, so the item shrinks to its own content's width instead, and `max-width` never gets a chance to apply. Confirmed precisely: raising `max-w-4xl` → `max-w-7xl` alone produced *zero* measured change. **Fixed by adding `w-full`** (`mx-auto w-full max-w-7xl ...`) — re-measured and the div correctly rendered at exactly 1280px this time.
    3. **Checked whether this was a wider, pre-existing bug before closing it out:** Learn's Index page uses the visually-identical `mx-auto max-w-4xl` pattern *without* `w-full` — worth checking rather than assuming it shares the bug. It doesn't: Learn's content div sits inside `LearnLayout`'s sidebar wrapper (`<div className="min-w-0 flex-1">`), which itself is a plain block element (no `flex` class), not a flex container — so the stretch/auto-margin interaction never triggers there. Confirmed via the same `getBoundingClientRect()` measurement (Learn's div correctly renders at its full 896px). No other page affected; nothing else to fix.
    - Re-verified after the real fix: `tsc --noEmit` + `eslint` clean; screenshots at 1920px, 1440px, and 390px (mobile unaffected, as expected — `max-width` only caps the upper bound) all confirm the page now genuinely uses the wider container, matching the reference's proportions; console-error check across `/practice`, `/practice/javascript-runtime`, `/practice/react` clean. Documented the `w-full`-inside-a-flex-`<main>` gotcha in `ui-registry.md` so a future page built directly under `AppLayout` doesn't repeat it. Dev server stopped via PID, `.next` cleared preemptively. Still not pushed.
  - **Follow-up 6 (`max-w-7xl` was too wide at 14"-laptop scale, same session, before any push/PR):** user compared against the same reference again — on a big monitor it looked right, but on a 14" laptop it now read as "too much wider," while the reference apparently reads correctly across big screens, 14" laptops, *and* tablets. `ui-rules.md`'s documented 1280px general-page standard (`max-w-7xl`, used in Follow-up 5) turned out to leave too little side margin at typical 14"-class logical resolutions (~1440–1512px) to read as comfortable, even though it looked fine at 1920px+. **Narrowed to `max-w-6xl` (1152px)** — re-checked explicitly at all 4 scales the user named rather than just the one that prompted the complaint (1920px monitor, 1512px 14"-laptop, 1024px and 820px tablet widths) plus mobile/console-error checks, all clean.
**Next after 28 finishes:** 29 Challenge Editor Page (Phase 10's numbered curriculum-expansion features are now complete — all 8 categories have a full Understand/Challenge/Interview/Build catalog — so Phase 5 (Practice) resumes per the scheduling note added after Feature 25; Simulate tab work for Phase 10's 68 new concepts continues separately via the unscheduled Simulator Backlog, not as a numbered feature).
