## Decisions Made During Build

- **00 Project Setup:** `create-next-app@latest` installed **Next.js 16.2.9** (React 19.2), not Next 15 as named in `architecture.md`. Followed the build-plan's literal `@latest` instruction rather than pinning to 15. Stack decision in `architecture.md` should be read as "Next.js 16" going forward.
  - Breaking change to plan for in **Feature 14 (Supabase Setup)**: Next 16 renames `middleware.ts`/`middleware()` to `proxy.ts`/`proxy()`. The `edge` runtime is not supported in `proxy` (runtime is fixed to `nodejs`). `architecture.md` and `build-plan.md` still say `middleware.ts` — use `proxy.ts` instead when implementing session validation.
  - Breaking change to plan for across all dynamic routes: `params`/`searchParams` (and `cookies()`/`headers()`) are async-only in Next 16 — always `await` them, no synchronous fallback.
  - Turbopack is on by default in 16; `next.config.ts` sets `turbopack.root` explicitly because a stray `package-lock.json` exists at `C:\Users\DEEPSHIKHA\` (outside this repo) which Next's workspace-root inference was picking up.
- **00 Project Setup:** The project folder name `Frontend-Forever` has capital letters, which `create-next-app` rejects as an invalid npm package name for the target directory. Worked around by scaffolding into a throwaway temp directory (lowercase name), moving the generated files into this root, and deleting the temp directory. `package.json` name is set to `frontend-forever`. No separate folder was kept — final project lives directly in this root as intended.
- **01 Design System:** `globals.css` defines the full `ui-tokens.md` token set verbatim in `@theme`, plus a small block of shadcn/ui primitive aliases (`--color-primary`, `--color-card`, `--color-muted`, `--color-ring`, etc., and `--radius-4xl` for `badge.tsx`'s pill shape) that point at our semantic tokens instead of shadcn's default oklch palette. This keeps installed primitives on-brand without ever editing files in `components/ui/`. If a newly-added shadcn component references a semantic class not yet aliased, add the alias in `globals.css` rather than hardcoding a color in the component.
- **01 Design System:** Theme persistence uses `useSyncExternalStore` in `ThemeProvider`, not `useState` + `useEffect`. An effect that calls `setState` synchronously to "correct" SSR state trips the `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` ESLint rule and is the wrong tool for syncing with an external mutable source (DOM class / localStorage) that can legitimately differ between server and client snapshots. `useSyncExternalStore`'s `getServerSnapshot` (`"light"`) vs `getSnapshot` (real DOM class) split avoids both the lint error and any hydration mismatch — the anti-FOUC script in `layout.tsx` (via `next/script`, not a raw `<script>` tag — raw tags inside a Server Component log a console error) sets the real class before hydration, and a `MutationObserver` notifies React of the change.
- **Pre-Phase-2 decision (after Phase 1 finished, before Feature 14 started): Auth provider switched from Supabase Auth to Better-Auth, "only for the authentication."** User-requested architecture change, not a bug fix. Supabase remains the hosted Postgres database (and Storage, if a future feature needs it) — only the auth/session/OAuth layer moves to Better-Auth. Resolved 3 open questions with the user before editing any docs:
  1. **Scope:** update every context doc that described Supabase as the auth provider, not just this file — `build-plan.md` (Phase 0 deps/env, Phase 2 features 14–17, Phase 3's RLS line), `architecture.md` (stack table, folder structure, OAuth/progress data flows, schema, Authentication, client patterns, invariants), `security.md`, `library-docs.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `error-handling.md`, `code-standards.md`, `project-overview.md` — all updated in this same pass so a future session reading any one of them doesn't get instructions that contradict this one.
  2. **DB adapter:** Better-Auth connects via a direct Postgres connection (`DATABASE_URL`, `pg`), not through `supabase-js`/PostgREST — it manages its own `user`/`session`/`account`/`verification` tables in the same Supabase-hosted Postgres instance. **Follow-up, same session:** user asked whether an ORM (Drizzle/Prisma) was in use — it wasn't (Kysely, a query builder, was the original pick, reusing the same library Better-Auth's own Postgres adapter is built on internally). Offered Kysely/Drizzle/Prisma as options; user chose **Drizzle**, so this was redone before any code existed: Better-Auth now uses `drizzleAdapter(db, { provider: 'pg' })` instead of a raw `Pool`, app queries go through Drizzle's query API (`db.query.*.findFirst()`, `db.insert().values().onConflictDoUpdate()`, etc.) instead of Kysely's `selectFrom`/`executeTakeFirst` builder syntax, and schema lives as TypeScript table definitions in `lib/schema/` migrated via `drizzle-kit` rather than Kysely's hand-written-SQL-plus-types approach. Every file touched in the first pass (`architecture.md`, `build-plan.md`, `security.md`, `library-docs.md`, `error-handling.md`, `code-standards.md`, this file) was re-edited in this same follow-up so nothing still shows Kysely syntax.
  3. **`profiles` linkage:** `profiles.id` now references Better-Auth's `user.id` instead of `auth.users` — Better-Auth is configured (`advanced.database.generateId`) to generate uuids so this PK stays consistent with every other table's uuid PKs, rather than Better-Auth's string-id default.
  - **The bigger architectural consequence, surfaced and resolved during this same pass, not left implicit:** RLS policies like `USING (user_id = auth.uid())` only work through Supabase's PostgREST layer with a Supabase-issued JWT. Since app data now also goes through a direct Postgres connection (reusing the same `lib/db.ts` pattern Better-Auth itself needs) rather than `supabase-js`, there is no per-request Supabase JWT for `auth.uid()` to read, and RLS doesn't apply to a direct connection anyway. Decided: **drop RLS as the authorization boundary entirely** — every query against a user-owned table must filter `WHERE user_id = session.user.id` in application code (`security.md`'s existing "defense in depth" language is now the *only* layer, not a second one on top of RLS). `DATABASE_URL` is documented everywhere as the new equivalent of the old `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` in sensitivity — full table access, no policy layer underneath.
  - **Why this was safe to do as a docs-only pass:** confirmed via `Glob` that no auth or DB code exists yet (`src/lib/` only has `utils.ts`, no `middleware.ts`/`proxy.ts`, no `app/(auth)/`, no `app/api/`) — Phase 1 was UI-only and Phase 2 hadn't started, so there was nothing to migrate or delete, only planning docs to correct before the first line of auth code gets written.
  - **Done in Feature 14** (was: "left for whoever builds Feature 14"): `@supabase/ssr`/`@supabase/supabase-js` uninstalled, `better-auth`/`drizzle-orm`/`pg` (+ `drizzle-kit`/`@types/pg` dev deps) installed. `.env.local` updated to the new var list (`NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL`/`NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` removed).
- **01 Design System:** Hit a stale Turbopack dev cache during verification — `globals.css` was fully rewritten but the served CSS still reflected the old shadcn token mapping (`.text-accent { color: var(--accent) }` instead of `var(--color-accent)`). Fixed by killing the dev server and deleting `.next` before restarting. Worth trying first if styles look wrong after an unusually large `globals.css` change and the file content looks correct on disk.
- **Pre-Phase-10 decision (after Feature 25 shipped, before Feature 26 started): added Phase 10 — Concept Curriculum Expansion to `build-plan.md`.** User observation that started it: every category has exactly 1 built concept (JS Runtime = Event Loop only), and even the concepts with a full 4-tab experience (React Rendering, CSS Specificity) are missing a written guide — the catalog looks far thinner than the platform's own Interview Prep collections imply. Four decisions resolved with the user up front:
  1. **Priority:** run this phase next, ahead of resuming Phase 5+ (Practice/Interview Prep/Roadmaps/Leaderboard/Premium) — those all lean on a real concept catalog behind them. **Refinement, same session, in two steps:** first, Feature 26 (Build Tab) — already "Next" before this decision — still runs *first*, since it's the last missing tab *system* and Phase 10 is about filling systems that already exist, not building new ones. **Second correction (user caught this, not initially planned for):** Feature 27 (Progress API + XP System) — the actual last feature of Phase 4, not Feature 26 — also has to run before Phase 10. `/api/progress` was deliberately built minimal in Feature 22 (writes only the tab-completion flag; Feature 27 layers on `xp_events`, `profiles.xp`, and streak updates) — every tab completion across every concept, including all of Phase 10's new ones, routes through it, so it needs to be complete before Phase 10 floods the catalog with content that would otherwise complete without earning XP. So the actual order is: 26 → 27 → Phase 10 → Phase 5+.
  2. **Simulate tab scope:** user chose "every concept eventually gets one" over the recommended "flagship concepts only." This makes Simulate open-ended — each simulator is a multi-day bespoke Framer Motion build (Features 09–12's precedent), so it is **not** one of Phase 10's numbered features; it's an ongoing backlog pulled from over time (prioritization rule: every category reaches 2 simulators before any category reaches 3), independent of the Understand/Challenge/Interview/Build content passes.
  3. **Scale:** decided category-by-category rather than one target count — JS Runtime and React ended up deepest (14 and 12 concepts) matching how real interview platforms weight those two; System Design stayed shortest (7) since there's little genuinely "beginner" content in frontend system design.
  4. **Concept list authorship:** user asked for a drafted proposal to react to, not a from-scratch brainstorm. Delivered as a reviewable artifact (not written into the repo until approved), then revised twice before being written into `build-plan.md`:
     - **Senior-engineer review pass** (explicit user ask: "review it like a senior engineer, don't miss important concepts"): caught 2 real teaching-order bugs (Event Loop was sequenced after Promises & Async/Await, backwards — promises are scheduled *by* the event loop; CSS Specificity was sequenced before Cascade & Inheritance, its own prerequisite) and ~15 real content gaps, the most significant being TypeScript jumping straight to Interfaces with no beginner on-ramp, React having no Forms concept despite it being one of the most common practical React interview tasks, JS Runtime never covering `==`/`===` coercion despite it already being a seeded interview question, and Browser Internals having nothing on CORS or web security (XSS/CSRF/CSP) despite this project's own `security.md` leaning on exactly that knowledge.
     - **Follow-up catch (user-reported):** Callbacks & Higher-Order Functions was missing from JS Runtime entirely — not a minor gap, since it's the prerequisite for Array & Object Methods (map/filter/reduce *are* the callback pattern) and the motivating problem Promises exist to solve. Inserted after Closures, which also reordered Array & Object Methods to come after it rather than before.
  - **Final approved curriculum: 76 concepts (8 existing + 68 proposed)** across all 8 categories, transcribed in full into `build-plan.md`'s Phase 10 section (Features 41–48, one per category) — that section, not any external draft, is now the durable source of truth.
  - **Numbering constraint surfaced and resolved:** feature numbers are tied to git branch names (`feature/[number]-[name]`) throughout this file's history, so a new phase can only be *numbered* at the end (40+) even though it's *scheduled* to run before Phase 5 — build-plan.md's top-level "Build order" line and the Phase 10 section both carry a note explaining this so a future session doesn't assume numbering order means execution order.
- **41 JavaScript Runtime Concepts: a platform-wide premium-gating proposal was raised and deliberately deferred to Feature 38, not built here.** The user proposed a broader rule mid-feature — beginner concepts fully free across all 5 tabs, intermediate/advanced concepts fully locked across all 5 tabs, platform-wide, for every category. Surfaced back to the user rather than folded into this feature: Feature 41 was scoped as content-only (zero code changes), the rule contradicts the currently-documented gate list in `project-overview.md`/`security.md` (which gates specific things — FF System Design questions, Build tab, Study Plans, Company Collections — not "every tab of every intermediate+ concept"), and only Build tab actually has premium lock/unlock UI today — Understand/Simulate/Challenge/Interview have no concept-level gate in the code at all. **Decision: keep Feature 41 content-only, using only the existing per-item `isPremium` mechanism (already exercised in `PROJECT_BRIEFS`/`CHALLENGES`, though only Build's was ever seen by a real user before this feature); Feature 38 (Premium Content Gating) should implement the user's rule — new gating code across the 4 ungated tabs, applied platform-wide by difficulty — when that feature comes up.** `build-plan.md`'s Feature 38 section should get a note reflecting this the next time it's touched.
- **42 Browser Internals Concepts: two of Feature 41's per-tab content rules were overridden, effective from this feature forward through Features 43–48 (not retroactive to Features 40/41's already-shipped content).**
  1. **Challenge is now mandatory for every concept, not selective.** Feature 41 skipped a Challenge for 2 of its 13 concepts ("not a natural executable-code exercise"). The user rejected that precedent outright — every concept gets one, in this feature and every remaining category. Where a concept isn't naturally a "write a function" exercise (CORS, web security, the network stack, service workers, Web Workers), the Challenge reframes the browser mechanic as a pure-logic problem the sandbox can assert against — the same technique Feature 41's `render-blocking-analyzer` project brief already used, just applied to Challenge too.
  2. **Premium is now concept-difficulty-driven, uniformly across every tab** (beginner → free everywhere, intermediate/advanced → premium everywhere), replacing Feature 41's per-tab rules (Challenge: only-advanced-premium; Interview: always-free regardless of difficulty). This is effectively pulling forward the *data* half of the platform-wide rule Feature 38 will eventually enforce with real gating UI — `concepts.isPremium` is used for real for the first time (previously `false` on every concept ever seeded). **Explicitly forward-only, per the user:** the original 8 pre-Phase-10 concepts and all 13 Feature 41 JS Runtime concepts are untouched, still `isPremium: false` at the concept level — "we'll take care of it in the future" if a retroactive pass is ever wanted.
  - **`browser-rendering-pipeline` (this feature's one pre-existing concept) was treated like Feature 41 treated `event-loop`:** already shipped with `isPremium: false`, left untouched rather than forced premium by its `intermediate` difficulty; its one new addition (a Challenge, `classify-style-change`) matches that at `isPremium: false` too, so the concept doesn't end up free with one premium-gated tab.
  - **Scope correction found mid-feature:** `browser-rendering-pipeline` turned out to have **no Understand MDX guide at all** — only `javascript-runtime` had any content on disk before this feature; the "existing concept, just add the missing Challenge" assumption from planning was wrong. This feature wrote all 9 Understand guides, not 8.
  - **Verified for real, not just by inspection**, following Feature 41's `find-leaked-listeners` precedent: a standalone Node harness mirroring `buildSandboxDoc.ts`'s exact `assert`/`assertEqual`/`delay` semantics ran all 72 real test assertions against the 17 new Challenge/Build `solutionCode`s (all passed), plus a deliberately wrong solution confirmed to fail (ruling out a vacuous harness). `npx tsx scripts/seed.ts` — 76 concepts (unchanged), 24 challenges (15 → 24), 144 interview questions (104 → 144), 25 project briefs (17 → 25); re-ran to confirm idempotency (identical counts). `tsc --noEmit` + `eslint` clean. Live dev server (cleared `.next` first, per Feature 40's documented stale-cache gotcha): spot-checked a free concept (`dom-vs-bom`) and a premium one (`web-workers-concurrency`) — all 4 tabs render real content on both, no "coming soon" fallback, no console/server errors.
- **Pre-Feature-28 decision (2026-07-10): restructured Feature 28 (Practice) from a single flat filterable list into a two-level Hub → Category List flow, before any Phase 5 code was written.** User shared BFE.dev screenshots (its Practice mega-dropdown of 9 categories with counts, a per-category filtered question list, and a split-pane problem/editor page) as a structural reference, explicitly critiquing its visual execution — dense gray text rows, ad slots crammed into the editor page — as "not looking good," while confirming the underlying flow (browse by category → filtered list → editor) was worth keeping. User then explicitly delegated the remaining design/engineering judgment calls ("you know better than me what is good for the platform and for the users") rather than specifying every detail.
  - **Decisions made under that delegated judgment:** (1) drive the hub off FF's own existing 8 concept categories (the same taxonomy already powering Learn) rather than inventing a parallel one — category cards reuse Feature 20 Learn Index's visual pattern (icon + name + count + completion %); (2) challenges render as cards (title, difficulty badge, concept tag, completion check), not BFE's plain numbered link rows; (3) the editor page's left panel (Feature 29) becomes tabbed (Description/Hints/Test Cases) instead of BFE's always-visible cluttered icon-button row.
  - **Ad slot:** user's own idea — in the future, once real traffic exists, add a single Google AdSense placement styled as one tasteful premium/sponsor banner, explicitly not BFE's scattered "ads via Carbon" look. Recorded as a deferred future note directly in Feature 29's spec; no placeholder ad UI is built now.
  - `build-plan.md`'s Feature 28 section rewritten to spec both the Hub (`/practice`) and Category List (`/practice/[category]`) pages under one feature (no new feature number needed — both are browse/list UI, same scope class as other multi-page features like Feature 21's shell+sidebar); Feature 29 updated with the tabbed left panel and the future ad-slot note. No code written yet — planning-only pass, done ahead of Feature 28 actually starting.
- **Post-Feature-47 decision (2026-07-09): added Phase 11 — Simulator Reimagining to `build-plan.md`, explicitly long-term/unscheduled, not a redirect of current work.** Started from the user's observation that the platform has drifted toward a text-guide + LeetCode-style-Challenge + Interview-Q&A model, away from the original "see concepts working, playful, game-like" founding idea. Confirmed the gap is real, not just a feeling: only 4 of 76 concepts have any Simulate tab at all (Features 09–12), and even those 4 are scripted walkthroughs (Play/Step/Restart/Speed over one fixed scenario) rather than something manipulable. Compared against paperdraw.dev (a system-design tool where users build/break their own architecture and watch live metrics react) and directly against `designs/hero-section-1-react-rendering.png` — the shipped hero simulator is a clean SaaS-dashboard diagram (thin-border cards, dotted arrows), its "Real DOM" panel is a static text mockup, and there's no code editor in it anywhere. The vision the user described: a real code editor as input, the internal pipeline animated interactively (game/video-like, pausable, pokeable) rather than steps highlighting in sequence, and a genuine rendered-output canvas showing the real page live — all in an illustrated/cartoonish "white paper canvas" style, not the current SaaS-panel look. **Explicitly confirmed as long-term**, not something to act on now — Phase 10 and the Simulator Backlog continue unchanged. Written into `build-plan.md` as Phase 11 with no numbered features yet, so the direction isn't lost but also doesn't block or reorder anything currently in flight.

## Notes

- **`scripts/seed.ts` doesn't invalidate a running dev server's Next.js Data Cache — a freshly-seeded (or renamed) concept can 404 or show stale data until the server restarts (found during Feature 40's rollout, not specific to that feature's content).** User reported a brand-new concept page 404ing right after Feature 40 seeded it, plus an unrelated-looking auth glitch (appeared logged out, logging back in bounced to the homepage still showing "Log In"), both on a dev server that had been running continuously across several reseeds.
  - **Root cause (confirmed, not assumed):** `getConceptBySlug`/`getConceptCatalog` (`features/learn/lib/queries/concepts.ts`) are wrapped in `unstable_cache(..., { tags: ["concepts"], revalidate: 3600 })`. `scripts/seed.ts` is a standalone Node process that writes straight to Postgres via its own `pg` pool — it has no request/server-action context, so it has no way to call Next's `revalidateTag("concepts")` on an already-running dev server. If a slug was queried (e.g. by anyone clicking around) *before* the seed added/renamed it, that miss (or stale row) is cached for up to an hour. Verified directly: `curl` to the 404ing route reproduced it live while a fresh DB query confirmed the row existed correctly (right slug, right category) — so this was a stale-cache-read problem, not a data problem.
  - **Fix:** restart the dev server after running `scripts/seed.ts` while it's already running — this clears Next's in-memory Data Cache (a `.next` clear isn't required for this specific issue, that's the separate Turbopack filesystem-cache problem documented elsewhere in this file, though clearing it too is harmless). Restarting resolved both the concept 404 and the auth glitch in one step, consistent with both being downstream of the same long-running, increasingly-stale server process rather than two separate bugs.
  - **If this resurfaces:** after any `npx tsx scripts/seed.ts` run, restart whatever `next dev` process is already running before testing the new/changed content — don't assume a real regression until you've done that.

- **User avatar sometimes shows fallback initials instead of the real OAuth photo, dev-only, not a code bug (found during Feature 26's verification, unrelated to that feature's own code):** user reported the Navbar showing initials ("SI") instead of their real Google profile photo after logging in. Diagnosed via the user's own DevTools Network tab screenshots rather than guessing — two requests fired to the *identical* `lh3.googleusercontent.com` avatar URL: the first `200 OK`, the second `429 Too Many Requests` — and the 429 came from Google's own image-serving infra (`Server: fife`), not this app.
  - **Root cause:** `@base-ui/react`'s `Avatar` (`useImageLoadingStatus`) detects load status by creating a hidden `new Image()` and setting `.src` inside a `useLayoutEffect`. React Strict Mode (on by default, dev-only) intentionally double-invokes every effect (mount → cleanup → mount again) to surface bugs — this creates that probe *twice* in quick succession for the same URL, firing two near-simultaneous real network requests. Google's CDN rejects the second one with a 429. Whichever of the two `Image()` instances React ends up keeping determines the final status shown; if that one lost the race, the Avatar renders its fallback initials instead of the photo.
  - **Confirmed dev-only, not a real app bug:** Strict Mode's double-effect-invocation doesn't happen in a production build (`next build && next start`), so this specific race can't occur once deployed. Verified the underlying DB/session data was correct throughout (`profiles.avatar_url` and Better-Auth's `user.image` both held a real, working Google photo URL) — this was purely a client-side dev-mode timing artifact, never a data problem.
  - **Why a plain refresh didn't immediately fix it:** the user had DevTools' Network tab "Disable cache" checkbox on (common while actively debugging) — that forces every reload to bypass the HTTP cache entirely, so the same race replayed on every single reload instead of the first successful response's `Cache-Control: public, max-age=86400` ever getting a chance to serve the second request from cache. Unchecking it and reloading normally resolved it immediately.
  - **If this resurfaces:** it's cosmetic-only (fallback initials, not a crash or missing data) and specific to `next dev` with DevTools cache disabled — don't assume a data/schema regression; check the Network tab for the same 200-then-429-from-`fife` pattern first.

- **Cross-cutting responsive breakpoint audit (same session as Feature 09, before starting Feature 10):** user tested the live site on real devices (14" laptop, iPad portrait/landscape, iPhone) via the local network and reported several layout problems with screenshots from each. Rather than guessing from the screenshots, measured every claim directly with Playwright at matching viewport sizes (1366×768/1512×982 laptop, 768×1024 tablet portrait, 1024×768 tablet landscape, 390×844 mobile) before changing anything — found real, precise root causes, not vague impressions:
  1. **Tablet landscape (1024px) page-level overflow + cramped navbar:** `Navbar.tsx`'s full desktop nav (pill + Follow-on-X/theme-toggle/Log-In group) switches on at `lg:` (1024px), but doesn't actually have room until well past that — measured the right-side button group alone extending 70px past the viewport edge at exactly 1024px. Fixed by raising all 4 of `Navbar.tsx`'s `lg:flex`/`lg:hidden` breakpoints to `xl:` (1280px), so the hamburger menu now covers the 768–1279px range instead of a cramped full nav.
  2. **Tablet portrait (768px) page-level overflow from company logos:** `CompanyLogosStrip.tsx`'s "no-scroll, centered" mode switched on at `sm:` (640px), but measured that 7 logos need ~960px to actually fit without overflowing — at 768px the row was 54px wider than its own box with `overflow-visible` set, bleeding into the page's real scroll width. Fixed by raising every `sm:` class in that row to `lg:` (1024px), matching where the row's own `scrollWidth`/`clientWidth` were empirically confirmed equal.
  3. **Mobile (390px) — the page could be scrolled horizontally, full stop.** Traced to `ConceptSwitcherTabs.tsx`, not the previously-assumed `CompanyLogosStrip` (past sessions' notes had misattributed a similar-looking number to the logos strip without re-verifying which element was actually responsible each time — worth remembering before citing an old "known baseline" as an explanation again). The tab group had zero overflow handling (`inline-flex`, no wrap) — measured its box at `left: -50, right: 440`, and `440` was the *exact* number matching `document.documentElement.scrollWidth`. Fixed by changing the container to `flex flex-wrap justify-center` with a `max-w-full`, and shrinking the pills' padding/font/icon size at the base breakpoint (`sm:` and up keeps the original sizing) — now wraps cleanly to 2 rows of 2 instead of forcing the page wider.
  4. **Laptop (1366×768 and similar shorter-viewport laptops) — the Event Loop simulator card (796px tall) was taller than the viewport (768px),** so no scroll position could show both the top of the card and the Play button at once. Fixed by trimming the simulator's own vertical footprint, not by hiding anything: `SimulatorPanel.tsx`'s 4 panels `min-h-[180px] p-4` → `min-h-[130px] p-3`, `CodePanel.tsx`/`OutputPanel.tsx` `p-4 leading-6` → `p-3 leading-5` (and `OutputPanel`'s `min-h-[120px]` → `min-h-[100px]`), `EventLoopSimulator.tsx`'s body `gap-4 p-5` → `gap-3 p-4`, `SimulatorControls.tsx` `gap-3 px-6 py-4` → `gap-2 px-4 py-3`. New card height: 678px — verified via `getBoundingClientRect()` (not just a screenshot) that it now fits inside a 768px viewport with 90px to spare, and confirmed visually that scrolling the card's top to y=20 leaves the Play button fully visible in the same frame.
  5. **Headline wrapping to 3 lines on mobile / an awkward 2-vs-3-line split on tablet portrait:** `Hero.tsx`'s `<h1>` was `text-4xl sm:text-5xl lg:text-5xl`. Changed the base size to `text-3xl` (mobile now wraps to a clean 2 lines instead of 3) and the `sm:` step to `text-4xl` instead of jumping straight to `text-5xl` (since `sm:` and `lg:` were previously identical, the headline got the full desktop size all the way down to 640px, including the entire 640–1023px tablet range where it didn't actually fit on one line) — `lg:text-5xl` (1024px+, true desktop) is unchanged from the original, already-approved Feature 03 sizing.
  - **Found but deliberately not touched, per explicit user instruction to only touch files relevant to the reported issues:** a small (10px) residual page overflow remains at exactly 768px tablet-portrait width, traced (via a refined check that excludes elements clipped by an ancestor's own `overflow-x-auto`/`hidden`, since a naive "widest element" search kept finding false positives inside already-correctly-scrolling containers) to a date span — `"Jun 2025"` — inside a `TestimonialsSection` card, an already-merged, unrelated Feature 07 component the user never mentioned or screenshotted. Flagged here for a future pass rather than fixed now.
  - **Two intermittent false alarms during verification, not real bugs:** (1) several screenshots came back fully blank below the navbar — confirmed as the same already-documented "first-hit Next.js dev cold-compile delay" / "instant JS-driven scroll gives the compositor no frames to repaint before the screenshot" artifacts noted elsewhere in this file, not application defects (re-screenshotting with a longer wait or a real `scrollIntoViewIfNeeded()` call always produced the correct result). (2) mobile's overflow check fluctuated between clean (390px) and as high as 719px across consecutive runs on the same unchanged code, mid-session, with no edits in between — matching this project's own previously-documented Turbopack-HMR-timing flakiness after a long run of rapid edits. Resolved by killing the dev server, clearing `.next`, and restarting clean (the same fix already used twice elsewhere in this file) — re-ran the exact same check immediately after and got a clean, repeatable 390=390 every time.
  - Re-verified after all fixes: typecheck/lint clean across every touched file; Playwright confirmed zero page-level horizontal overflow at laptop/tablet-landscape/mobile (tablet-portrait's only remaining 10px is the unrelated, out-of-scope `TestimonialsSection` item above); visually confirmed via screenshots at all 4 breakpoints that the navbar, headline, company logos, concept switcher, and simulator all read correctly.
  - These fixes touch `Navbar.tsx` (Feature 02), `Hero.tsx` and `ConceptSwitcherTabs.tsx` (Feature 03), and `CompanyLogosStrip.tsx` (Feature 04) — all already merged to `develop` — alongside `EventLoopSimulator`'s own files (Feature 09, still on the open `feature/09-event-loop-simulator` branch/PR). Committed and pushed together on that same branch/PR rather than split out, since the user is managing merges manually.
  - **Standing instruction from the user, going forward:** "every standard screen size" should be checked for every upcoming feature from here on — not just desktop and one mobile width as before.

- **Follow-up to item 4 above (later session, same feature branch):** the user manually increased `SimulatorPanel.tsx`'s 4 panels from `min-h-[130px]` to `min-h-[170px]` for more breathing room. Re-verified the laptop-viewport-fit constraint still holds: card height is now 718px (up from 678px), still fits inside a 768px-tall laptop viewport with the Play button visible at the bottom — confirmed via `getBoundingClientRect()` and a screenshot. No overflow introduced on mobile (390px). If this value needs to grow further, re-check against a 768px-tall viewport before assuming it still fits.

- **Follow-up, reversing item 1 above (later session, same feature branch):** the user explicitly rejected hiding the full nav behind a hamburger through the whole 1024–1279px tablet-landscape range and asked for the real nav, compacted to fit, instead. Reverted the show/hide breakpoint in `Navbar.tsx` back to `lg:` (1024px) and compacted everything that renders from `lg:` up (pill links, "Follow on X", Log In, logo) with `xl:` overrides restoring full desktop sizing at 1280px+ — see `ui-rules.md` → Responsive Design and `ui-registry.md` → Navbar for the exact values. Also fixed two issues caught by `/review`: (1) the icon-only "Follow on X" link had no accessible name at all between `lg:` and `xl:` (label text was `display:none`, icon was `aria-hidden`) — added `aria-label="Follow Frontend Forever on X"` to match the mobile menu's already-correct pattern; (2) the wordmark's visibility logic (`min-[1024px]:hidden min-[1100px]:inline`) only works because both bounds are arbitrary breakpoints — mixing in a named one (`lg:hidden`) silently fails to cascade by pixel order, confirmed by testing both ways — documented with an inline comment since this isn't discoverable by reading the class names alone. Verified zero overflow/clipping at 1024/1100/1279/1280px via Playwright screenshots, typecheck/lint clean, no console errors.

Pill border-radius (`rounded-2xl`), icon choices, and button styles were already correct against the design. Fixed in `Navbar.tsx` (`NavbarPillLink`, `NavbarMobileLink`, "Follow on X" anchor) and corrected the same wrong claim in `ui-rules.md`/`ui-registry.md`. Pixel-measured the design: `FF`'s cap-height is ~3x "Frontend Forever"'s. Changed logo to `text-3xl`/`text-sm` (was `text-2xl`/`text-lg`), pill container to `px-1.5 py-2.5` (was `p-1.5`) with `border-border-light` + `shadow-xl` (was `border-border` + `shadow-md`) for a softer, more diffused look. `border-border-light` (`#F3F4F6`) is an existing `ui-tokens.md` token, not a new one. - **02 Navbar + Footer (round 3):** User pointed out the landing page background and navbar background read as the same color. Re-checked the design pixel-by-pixel along the header's bottom edge (away from the pill) and found no border line or color step anywhere — the design's header has no chrome of its own; only the floating pill has a visible surface/border/shadow. Removed `border-b border-border` and changed the header from `bg-surface` to `bg-background` to match, so the page and header are intentionally the same color, exactly like the reference. (The header also already had `sticky top-5`, not `top-0` — navbar floats with a gap from the viewport edge — which is what made the previously-identical-but-technically-different `bg-surface`/`bg-background` colors visible as a seam in the first place.)
- **02 Navbar + Footer (round 4):** Round 3's fix used `--color-background` (#FAFAF9, off-white) on the assumption that the page and header should match each other. Pixel-sampling the full reference PNG (PowerShell `System.Drawing`, not just the header edge this time) showed the *entire* canvas — page margins, gaps between sections, everywhere — samples as pure `#FFFFFF`, identical to the pill's `bg-surface`. So `--color-background` was never actually used in the delivered design; the whole light-mode page is `bg-surface`. Changed `Navbar.tsx`'s header and `page.tsx`'s outer container from `bg-background` to `bg-surface` (scoped to these two files only — `--color-background`/`ui-tokens.md` left untouched, since other future pages may still legitimately want the off-white token; user chose this scoped fix over a global token change). Verified via Playwright screenshot of the running dev server — navbar, header, and page now read as one continuous white, matching the design.
  - Follow-up bug from the same round: `header` uses `sticky top-5`, and `position: sticky` immediately offsets an element away from the viewport edge to satisfy its `top` value — even at scroll position 0, with no scrolling needed. That offset leaves an empty gap in the document flow above the header, which is painted by whatever is behind it: `<body>`'s own background, still `bg-background` at the time. Fixed by changing `body { @apply bg-background ...}` to `bg-surface` in `globals.css`'s `@layer base` block — `<body>` is shared root chrome like the navbar, not per-page content, so this is consistent with the round-4 scoping (not a `--color-background` token edit). Needed another dev-server restart + `.next` cache clear to see the change (same stale-Turbopack-cache issue as the note in Feature 01) — re-verified via pixel sampling the new screenshot: gap and header both read `255,255,255`.
- **03 Hero Section (Static):** Ran the `architect` skill before building — aligned on "static" (full visual fidelity, zero interactive logic; entrance motion only) and resolved two scope decisions with the user: (1) the simulator panel placeholder is a full static replica of the design's Event Loop panel, not the build-plan's literal minimal "card with label + badge" wording, since design screenshots are the source of truth and Feature 09 will later swap in the real interactive engine on the same layout; (2) the 4 concept switcher tabs have no `onClick` in this feature — only Event Loop has any content, so making the other 3 "clickable" with nothing to switch to would be misleading. Real switching is Feature 13.

See `ui-registry.md` for full details.
  - Headline was first built at the build-plan's literal 56–72px and wrapped onto 2 lines — wrong against the design, which fits it on one line. Diagnosed by resizing the full-resolution design PNG (2731×4096) down to the screenshot's working width (1440) and comparing cropped regions directly, then iterating Tailwind size classes against fresh Playwright screenshots until it matched (down to `text-4xl sm:text-5xl lg:text-5xl` + `max-w-6xl`) — same iterative pixel-compare method as the Navbar fixes, not a one-shot calculation. `ui-rules.md` corrected.

Since `ui-tokens.md` has no generic "purple"/"blue" token, reused the closest existing semantic tokens (`premium`, `success`, `info`, `streak`) outside their original gamification meaning, scoped to this one component — documented in `ui-registry.md` so it isn't mistaken for those tokens' usual semantics elsewhere.

Also hit a stale dev-server-on-port-3000 from a previous session returning HTTP 500; killed it and started fresh (same category of issue as Feature 01's stale Turbopack cache — when a previously-started dev server misbehaves, kill and restart rather than debugging the stale instance).

Reverted `ConceptSwitcherTabs.tsx` to the pill-group style and corrected `ui-rules.md`/`ui-registry.md` to document this as a deliberate user-requested deviation from `designs/hero-section-1-event-loop.png`, not a mistake — the design's actual tab bar is still the continuous divided style, on record in case a future pass wants to revisit it.

Documented in `ui-registry.md`.
- **02 Navbar + Footer (round 5):** User flagged dark mode as flat/low-contrast and asked for a true black backdrop that's still easy on the eyes, with the navbar/buttons reading more visibly. Root cause: round 4 made `body`/`Navbar` header/`page.tsx` use plain `bg-surface` to pixel-match the light-mode reference (where the whole canvas is one white) — but that same single-token treatment carries into dark mode, where `--color-surface` (`#1A1A1A`) was the *only* tone in play, so the page, header, pill, and card all rendered identically with zero hierarchy (the light-mode design relies on `shadow-xl` for separation, which barely registers on a dark background). Light mode has a real reference screenshot proving "everything is one white" is correct; dark mode has no such reference, and the user's direct feedback is that dark mode specifically needs the opposite — a darker backdrop than its elevated surfaces, which is standard dark-theme practice (e.g. Material's elevation system) since shadows don't carry the separation job in the dark.
  - Fix: kept `bg-surface` in light mode but added `dark:bg-background` on `body` (`globals.css`), `Navbar.tsx`'s header, and `page.tsx`'s outer container — these three now diverge from the pill/card's `bg-surface` only in dark mode. Darkened `--color-background` (dark) from `#111111` to `#0A0A0A` for a true near-black that's still a hair off pure `#000000` (avoids the harsher max-contrast halation against light text that reads as "hurts the eyes"). Lightened the dark border tokens for visibility against the new darker backdrop: `--color-border` `#2A2A2A → #333333`, `--color-border-light` `#222222 → #292929`, `--color-border-muted` `#333333 → #404040`. `--color-surface`/`secondary`/`tertiary`/`elevated` untouched — they didn't need to change since contrast comes from the backdrop getting darker, not from them getting lighter. Mirrored all dark-token changes in `ui-tokens.md` and corrected `ui-rules.md`'s navbar section (still described the old single-token light+dark `bg-background` header from before round 4). Verified via Playwright screenshot of the dev server with `localStorage.theme = 'dark'` + pixel-sampling: header/page sample `#0A0A0A`, pill/card/footer sample `#1A1A1A` — a clear, deliberate two-tone split instead of one flat color.
  - Follow-up: user wanted the footer specifically to match the body/header backdrop in dark mode instead of standing out as a `bg-surface` block — added `dark:bg-background` to `Footer.tsx` alongside its existing light-mode `bg-surface`, same pattern as the header/body/page-container fix above. The `border-t border-border` is now the only thing separating footer from page content in dark mode (as in light mode). Re-verified via pixel sampling: footer samples `#0A0A0A`, matching body/header exactly.
- **04 Company Logos Strip:** Ran the `architect` skill before building. Two decisions resolved with the user up front: (1) pixel-inspecting `designs/hero-section-1-event-loop.png` showed the logo strip actually sits *inside* the hero flow, between the CTAs and the concept switcher tabs — not as a separate page-level section below the whole hero block, which is what the build-plan's feature-by-feature numbering implies. Built it that way (editing `Hero.tsx` from Feature 03 to insert `<CompanyLogosStrip />` and shifting the existing motion stagger for tabs/simulator/scroll-indicator back by ~0.4s to make room), matching the design over the literal build-plan structure — same precedent as Feature 03's own headline-size and tab-style corrections. (2) Feature 03 isn't merged to `develop` yet (no PR opened), so `feature/04-company-logos-strip` branches off `feature/03-hero-section` instead of `develop`, since `Hero.tsx` only exists there.
  - The 6 logos (build-plan listed Google, Meta, Amazon, Microsoft, Stripe, Airbnb) are pixel-sampled as **monochrome** in the design (near-black, `text-text-primary`), not the companies' real brand colors — built as simplified inline-SVG/text components in `src/components/shared/CompanyLogos.tsx` using `currentColor`, not static files in `public/logos/` as build-plan literally says, so they correctly invert in dark mode. Same reasoning as `XLogo` from Feature 02.
  - Caught during visual verification (not by typecheck/lint, which both passed clean before this fix): on mobile the logo row silently overflowed the *page* instead of scrolling internally. Root cause — `Hero.tsx`'s section is `flex flex-col items-center`, and the wrapping `<div>` around `CompanyLogosStrip` (and the component's own root `<div>`) lacked `w-full`; without it, a flex item's cross-axis size shrinks to its content's intrinsic width instead of the viewport's, so the scrollable row never actually became wider than its own box and nothing scrolled. Fixed by adding `w-full` at both levels, matching the pattern `HeroSimulatorPreview`'s wrapper already used. Verified via Playwright screenshots (desktop light/dark, mobile 390px) — confirmed via direct DOM measurement (`scrollWidth` 671 vs `clientWidth` 342 on mobile) before and after the fix, not just visually. Any new full-bleed child added to `Hero.tsx` needs the same `w-full` treatment or it will hit this same silent-overflow bug.
  - **Follow-up:** user supplied real brand SVGs one at a time post-build and asked for each placeholder logo to be swapped in (recolored to `currentColor` to stay monochrome): Google, Meta's icon, Amazon, Stripe all replaced with real path data. One supplied "Meta wordmark" SVG was caught as garbled/mismatched before integration — rendering it standalone first showed it actually spelled "heton" with an unrelated two-circle icon, not "Meta" — so it was rejected rather than wired in; render any future supplied SVG standalone before trusting it. Airbnb was then removed entirely and replaced with Anthropic (real SVG, `fillRule="evenodd"`) per explicit user request, and Cursor was added as a 7th logo (icon + wordmark, two real SVGs) on top of the build-plan's original 6 — the row is now Google, Meta, Amazon, Microsoft, Stripe, Anthropic, Cursor, diverging significantly from build-plan's original 6-company list. All 7 still fit on one line on desktop and the mobile scroll/mask treatment still holds — re-verified visually after each addition.
  - **Follow-up (sizing bug):** user asked if there was an alignment issue. Measured via `getBoundingClientRect()` in the live DOM rather than eyeballing — all 7 logos were already correctly vertically centered (`items-center` working as intended, centers within 2px of each other), but rendered heights ranged from 16px (Anthropic, Cursor, both `h-4`) to 20px (Google, Amazon, Stripe, already `h-5`) to 28px (Meta, Microsoft — driven by Tailwind's `text-xl` default 28px line-height on their text labels, not their icon size). The 28px outliers were fixed by adding `leading-none` to Meta's and Microsoft's text spans, collapsing them back to ~20px to match the rest of the row. Anthropic and Cursor were left at 16px in the final pass — smaller than the other 5 by deliberate choice during cleanup, not an unresolved bug. See `ui-registry.md` for current per-logo sizing and how to bump those two to match if a future pass wants full uniformity.
  - **Follow-up (`/review`):** ran the review skill before PR. Found 3 issues, all fixed:
    1. `CompanyLogos.tsx` bundled 7 unrelated components in one file, against `code-standards.md`'s "one component per file" rule and the project's own `XLogo.tsx` precedent. Split into `src/components/shared/logos/` — one file per logo (`GoogleLogo.tsx`, `MetaLogo.tsx`, `AmazonLogo.tsx`, `MicrosoftLogo.tsx`, `StripeLogo.tsx`, `AnthropicLogo.tsx`, `CursorLogo.tsx`).
    2. `AmazonLogo`'s hardcoded `id="amazon-logo-a"` was a latent DOM-id-collision risk if the shared component were ever rendered twice on one page. Replaced with React's `useId()` so each render gets a guaranteed-unique id.
    3. The logo row was almost entirely invisible to screen readers — verified by reading the live accessibility tree, not just visually: 5 of 7 logos had zero accessible name (pure decorative SVGs with no fallback), and the other 2 leaked their visible text inconsistently. Fixed by making every logo's own markup fully `aria-hidden` and adding one `<span className="sr-only">{name}</span>` per logo in `CompanyLogosStrip.tsx`, so every brand name is announced exactly once regardless of how that logo is built internally. Re-verified via DOM inspection (each wrapper has exactly one un-hidden `sr-only` name + one `aria-hidden` logo root) and confirmed zero visual regression via screenshot diff.
- **05 How It Works Section:** Ran the `architect` skill before building. User explicitly asked whether this section's motion was inspired by Notion/Linear's interactive marketing sections — it wasn't (every existing homepage section just uses `whileInView` + manual delay stagger) — and chose to elevate it: scroll-linked motion via Framer Motion's `useScroll`/`useTransform`, the first use of that pattern in this codebase, over the alternative of adding hover micro-interactions on top of the existing simple stagger (explicitly deferred, to keep scope contained). Two other decisions resolved with the user up front: (1) keep the section's height natural/compact like other sections rather than artificially tall to stretch out the scroll-scrubbed reveal; (2) keep a connector between steps on the mobile vertical stack (reoriented vertically) rather than dropping it, since the connecting motif is what signals "this is a sequence."
  - No design screenshot exists for this section (`designs/` only has hero variants + Learn page) — built directly from `build-plan.md`'s text spec: 5 steps (Understand/Simulate/Challenge/Interview/Build), each `icon + title + 1-line description`, no card background, horizontal-with-arrows on desktop / vertical-stack on mobile.
  - **Scroll-offset bug caught during verification, not by typecheck/lint:** the first `useScroll({ offset: ["start 0.8", "end 0.35"] })` attempt made the last step (and its connector) permanently stuck at `opacity: 0` no matter how far down the page was scrolled. Root cause: this is currently the last homepage section before a short `Footer` (Features 06–08 don't exist yet), so the page's total scrollable distance ran out before the section's bottom edge could physically reach 35% from the top of the viewport — the math demanded more scroll room than the page had. Diagnosed by reading each step's live inline `style.opacity`/`style.transform` via Playwright at a series of `window.scrollTo()` positions (not by eyeballing screenshots), which showed steps revealing correctly up to a hard ceiling that matched the page's actual max scroll position. Fixed by loosening the offset to `["start 0.9", "end 0.7"]`, which completes well within the currently-available scroll room with margin to spare. If a future section is added between this one and the footer, this has more headroom than it needs today — but if this `useScroll` pattern is reused on another section that also happens to sit near the bottom of a short page, re-verify the same way rather than assuming the same offset values transfer.
  - Confirmed via Playwright DOM inspection that this feature introduces no overflow: the only `scrollWidth > clientWidth` offenders on mobile belong to Feature 04's `CompanyLogosStrip` (its intentional internal `overflow-x-auto` scroll row), not to anything in `HowItWorksSection`.
  - **Follow-up (full redesign, same session, before any PR):** after the v1 layout above was built and verified, the user shared a screenshot of a Notion marketing-site section ("Keep work moving 24/7") — a left vertical list of selectable items (one expanded with a description) paired with a right-side live-preview panel that swaps content per selection — and asked to build something like that instead. Three scope decisions resolved with the user before rebuilding: (1) replace the v1 row/stack layout entirely rather than keep both (the row and the switcher cover the same ground; keeping both had no clear justification); (2) the right-side preview content per step is a simple static placeholder mockup for each step (no real simulator/challenge/interview UI exists yet — those are Features 09–26), not a reuse of `HeroSimulatorPreview`'s specific visual frame; (3) no autoplay/pause control for this pass — click-to-select only, matching "UI first" and keeping this PR's scope contained; autoplay is a possible later fast-follow.
    - The entire v1 implementation (scroll-linked `useScroll`/`useTransform`, the 9-slot domino reveal, `StepItem`/`StepConnector`) was deleted, not kept behind a flag or commented out — it no longer matches the chosen interaction model (select-one-see-its-demo vs. reveal-all-in-sequence), so there was nothing in it worth preserving. `HowItWorksSection.tsx` is now a two-panel selector shell; the 5 step "Preview" components live in a new `homepage/how-it-works/` subfolder (one file each, same precedent as `shared/logos/`), each a small static mockup foreshadowing its eventual real tab from `build-plan.md` (Feature 09's engine, Feature 24's Challenge Tab, Feature 25's Interview Tab, Feature 26's Build Tab) so that swapping in the real thing later only touches one file, not the switcher shell.

  - **Follow-up 2 (second full redesign, same session, before any PR — v2 → v3):** user said v2 still wasn't it, then described the actual target in detail: 5 separate full sections (not one switcher card), alternating left/right per step, colorful animated SVG visuals, and "texts moving doing something" — specifically per-letter kinetic typography where a word's two repeated letters (e.g. "Understand"'s two `d`s) fly past each other in a parabolic arc and swap places, with other letters flipping/rotating in. Ran the `architect` skill's question-by-question style directly (no separate subagent) since the bulk of remaining unknowns were creative/scope decisions only the user could resolve, not codebase unknowns. Resolved up front: (1) **no GSAP** — `architecture.md` commits to Framer Motion only, and the user's own "shouldn't hang, lightweight" requirement argues against a second animation runtime when Framer Motion already covers everything described; (2) **no bespoke character illustrations** — Notion's actual illustrations are commissioned art, not something reproducible via hand-written SVG; built simple custom shape/icon compositions in brand colors instead (upgraded v2's preview cards rather than starting over); (3) **one generic letter-swap algorithm, not 5 manually curated word choreographies** — among a word's repeated letters, swap the pair with the largest gap between occurrences (tie-broken by whichever starts later) — verified by hand-tracing all 5 words that this mechanically reproduces the user's own "Understand" example (the two `d`s) with zero special-casing; (4) one-shot `whileInView` entrances per section, not continuous scroll-linked `useScroll` — directly answers "shouldn't hang" and avoids re-hitting the exact class of scroll-offset fragility already learned the hard way in v1.
    - v2's entire two-panel switcher shell (`activeId` state, `StepListItem`, the `AnimatePresence mode="wait"` crossfade) was deleted — same "no value preserving code that doesn't match the chosen interaction model" reasoning as the v1→v2 transition. The 5 `*Preview` components were kept and upgraded (enlarged, given one motion touch each) since their actual visual content was already approved at small scale, not thrown away.
    - **Real bug caught during mobile verification, not visually obvious from most screenshots:** the first letter-animation implementation gave each individual letter its own `whileInView`/`viewport` trigger. On a mobile screenshot, "Simulate" rendered as "ulate" — its first 3 letters ("S", "i", "m") were permanently invisible. Direct DOM inspection (`getComputedStyle`/inline `style.opacity`/`getBoundingClientRect`, the same technique used for the v1 scroll-offset bug) showed those letters frozen at `opacity: 0` with `transform: rotateX(-90deg)` and, critically, a **rendered bounding-box height of exactly 0px** — `rotateX(-90deg)` with no `perspective` set fully foreshortens the element's projected height to zero. A zero-area element can fail to ever register as "intersecting" with an IntersectionObserver, so `whileInView` never fired for those specific letters — non-deterministically, since sibling letters on the exact same line (`u`,`l`,`a`,`t`,`e`) happened to trigger fine. This is the kind of bug that "looks fine" in most screenshots (other words/breakpoints didn't happen to hit it) and only showed up reliably via direct state inspection. Fixed by restructuring `AnimatedHeading` to put the `whileInView`/`viewport` trigger **only on the parent container** (always a healthy, non-degenerate bounding box) and propagating the reveal to letters via Framer Motion `variants` + `transition: { staggerChildren }` — the idiomatic pattern for staggering a group of children, and one that never depends on a tiny/transformable child correctly self-reporting its own visibility. Also softened the flip angle from `-90deg` to `-60deg` as a second safety margin. Re-verified via the same direct-DOM-inspection method (not just re-screenshotting) that all letters in all 5 words settle to `opacity: 1` on both mobile and desktop. **Lesson for any future per-letter/per-item entrance animation in this codebase: observe the parent, never each tiny child.**
    - Re-verified: typecheck/lint clean; Playwright screenshots desktop/mobile × light/dark for all 5 sections confirming correct alternation (text/visual flips every other step) and settled letter animations; repeated the mobile `scrollWidth`/`clientWidth` overflow check (one transient 719px reading turned out to be a hot-reload artifact caught mid-recompile — re-running the identical check immediately after gave a clean, consistent 440px, the same pre-existing `CompanyLogosStrip` baseline as v1/v2, confirming no real regression).
  - **Follow-up 3 (visual-polish pass on v3, same session, before any PR):** user rejected the colorless v3 outright as "ugly" and said to use my own judgment rather than asking more clarifying questions. Diagnosed the likely cause directly rather than re-asking: every preview card used the same flat `border border-border bg-surface-secondary` treatment regardless of step, and every icon/accent was plain black or one repeated teal — reading as a gray settings-panel UI, not a colorful illustrated landing page, despite the right alternating structure. Fix: assigned each of the 5 steps one of the existing semantic tokens purely for hue — Understand=`accent`, Simulate=`info`, Challenge=`premium`, Interview=`success`, Build=`streak` — same "reuse a semantic token outside its literal meaning, scoped to one component" precedent already established by `HeroSimulatorPreview`'s 4-color column treatment in Feature 03. Applied that theme color to: a new large rounded icon badge per section (`size-16 rounded-2xl`, theme `bg-{color}-light/muted` + `text-{color}`), each preview card's outer shell (swapped flat `border + bg-surface-secondary` for borderless `bg-{color}-light/muted` + `shadow-sm`, dropping borders project-wide in this section in favor of tint+elevation), a new decorative blurred color blob (`blur-3xl opacity-20`) behind each visual panel for depth, and the heading's swap-letters (now rendered in the step's theme color instead of plain black, tying the motion and color systems together). Semantic status colors inside cards (pass/fail green/red, "Marked as understood" check) were deliberately left alone — only the card shell, badge, blob, and swap-letters take the theme color.
    - Re-verified: typecheck/lint clean; Playwright screenshots desktop/mobile × light/dark confirming each step reads as a distinct, vibrant color family in both themes; repeated the overflow check — same intermittent 440/510/627/719 fluctuation as before, traced this time (not just re-run-and-hope) to the exact same pre-existing `CompanyLogosStrip` logo elements every single time across 5 repeated fresh loads, never anything from this redesign, confirming it's a known dev-server (Turbopack HMR/style-injection) timing race already documented elsewhere in this file, not a regression.
  - **Follow-up 4 (second polish pass on v3, same session, before any PR; Auto Mode active — proceeded on reasonable judgment without re-asking):** user gave three pieces of feedback at once: (1) section-to-section alignment looked inconsistent; (2) still needed more "creative and illustration," not just colored cards; (3) wanted continuous animation "every 2 second" instead of one-shot scroll reveals, for a more attractive/interactive feel.
    - **(1) Alignment root cause:** `StepSection`'s root flex container used `items-center`, vertically centering the text column against the visual column. Since `SimulatePreview` is taller than the other 4 preview cards (extra status row + controls row), centering shifted that section's icon badge/heading to a different vertical start point than the other 4 sections, which all centered against shorter, more similar-height cards — reading as "randomly" inconsistent even though every section uses the identical structure. Fixed by changing to `lg:items-start` so every section's text and visual columns share a top edge regardless of content-height differences on either side. Confirmed via screenshot comparison across all 5 steps that the icon badge and the top of each preview card now align consistently.
    - **(2) More illustration:** added two small `aria-hidden` floating decorative shapes per section (a `Sparkles` icon top-right and a `Plus` icon bottom-left of each visual panel, both in the step's theme color) — a deliberately lightweight way to add "illustrated" visual interest without bespoke art (still no GSAP, no external assets, consistent with constraints already settled in the v2→v3 redesign).
    - **(3) Continuous motion:** added the first **infinite-loop** (not one-shot `whileInView`) Framer Motion animations in this codebase — `animate` (not `whileInView`) with `transition: { duration: 2, repeat: Infinity }` on: each icon badge's icon (gentle scale+rotate), the two floating decorations (float/rotate), and one signature accent per preview card (Understand's lightbulb pulse, Simulate's dot now loops back-and-forth forever instead of animating once, Challenge's code block gained a blinking text cursor + pulsing pass/fail badges, Interview's thumbs-up wiggles, Build's terminal icon wiggles + button pulses). Kept deliberately small in count (1–3 looping elements per section, simple transform/opacity tweens only) specifically because of the project's own "shouldn't hang, lightweight" requirement from earlier in this same redesign — infinite loops are more expensive than one-shot animations by definition (they never stop), so scope was kept tight rather than animating everything continuously.
    - Re-verified: typecheck/lint clean; Playwright screenshots desktop/mobile × light/dark confirming consistent alignment and visible decorations/loops; repeated the overflow check (clean 440px baseline, same as always, the new absolutely-positioned decorations don't escape their relative parent).

Checked first whether I could generate matching art myself — confirmed I have no image-generation tool in this environment, only code editing — and researched where this style typically comes from (IconScout's and Flaticon's "hand drawn doodle" collections, ~20k+ icons each) since grabbing assets from those marketplaces without the user picking a specific licensed pack wasn't something I should do unilaterally. User opted to generate all 5 icons externally themselves (lightbulb, sync arrows, trophy, chat bubble, wrench) using an AI image tool, using color specs I gave matching the existing per-step theme tokens (e.g. `#0D9488` teal for Understand, `#2563EB` blue for Simulate, etc.), and placed the resulting files in `public/icons/how-it-works/`.
    - The 5 delivered files were genuine vector SVGs (verified by inspecting for embedded base64 raster data — none found) but each 104–196 KB, far larger than a normal icon SVG. Inspecting unique fill counts (82 for the bulb vs. 900–1100+ for the other 4) showed why: the bulb is pure grayscale (no color at all, inconsistent with the brief), and the other 4 lean toward their correct theme hue but have hundreds-to-thousands of near-duplicate fill shades — an artifact of however the source was vectorized/traced, not deliberate shading. Flagged both findings to the user rather than silently working around them: asked whether to accept the monochrome bulb as-is or have it regenerated (kept as-is, reads fine against the teal badge) — the hue-but-not-flat-color issue on the other 4 wasn't blocking enough to need a decision, just noted.
    - Wired all 5 into `HowItWorksSection.tsx`'s icon badges via `next/image` with the `unoptimized` prop — Next's built-in image optimizer rejects SVG input by default for security (`dangerouslyAllowSVG` would require a `next.config.ts` change with real XSS tradeoffs for a feature that doesn't need raster optimization anyway, since vectors don't benefit from format conversion/resizing); `unoptimized` sidesteps that while still satisfying `eslint-config-next`'s `no-img-element` rule, which a plain `<img>` would have failed. Removed the lucide `Lightbulb`/`ArrowLeftRight`/`Trophy`/`MessageCircle`/`Wrench` imports and the `icon: LucideIcon` field from `STEPS` entirely — replaced with `iconSrc`/`iconWidth`/`iconHeight` per step (the floating decorative `Sparkles`/`Plus` from the previous follow-up stayed as lucide icons, untouched).
    - **Performance follow-through, not just acceptance:** ~780 KB total across 5 decorative icons was flagged as worth fixing given this redesign's running "shouldn't hang, lightweight" theme — ran `npx svgo --precision 2` on all 5 files in place, a lossless-for-this-purpose optimization (reduces coordinate precision and redundant path data, not visual fidelity at any realistic display size). Got 86–92% reduction per file, ~108 KB total. Verified via before/after Playwright screenshots at the actual rendered badge size (`h-9 w-auto`, ~24–36px) that nothing visibly changed — re-ran the full 5-icon × desktop/dark screenshot sweep, zero console errors, typecheck/lint clean.

Replaced the `size-16 rounded-2xl bg-{color}-light` badge with: an `absolute size-28 rounded-full opacity-40 blur-2xl` glow (same recipe as the visual panel's blob, just smaller) sitting behind a much larger bare icon (`h-20 md:h-24`, up from `h-9`). `BADGE_CLASSES` stayed in the file (still used by the floating `Sparkles`/`Plus` decorations) — only the per-step icon's own container changed.

Isolated by elimination, in order: (1) checked the sibling description paragraph two lines below, using the identical `whileInView`/`viewport={{ once: true, margin: "-80px" }}` pattern — it triggered fine, ruling out a general viewport-trigger breakdown; (2) suspected the nested continuously-animating icon (`motion.span` with `animate` + `repeat: Infinity`) was somehow interfering with the parent's own trigger — temporarily stripped it entirely, bug persisted, ruling that out too; (3) suspected the `margin: "-80px"` itself, since this is a notably smaller/shorter element than anything else in this codebase using that same convention — removed it (now just `viewport={{ once: true }}`), bug fixed immediately, confirmed across all 5 steps via the same inline-style check. Root cause inside Framer Motion/the browser's IntersectionObserver is unconfirmed (most likely an edge case where a -80px-inset root margin interacts badly with a small/short element on a short viewport), but the fix is verified and isolated to just this one element — every other `whileInView` in the codebase keeps its `margin: "-80px"` unchanged. **If a small, short, or low-on-the-page element ever silently fails to animate in on mobile only, try dropping its `margin` before assuming the bug is elsewhere in the component.**
